Fortune (श्री/wealth) and prosperity.
In the compilation (संग्रह/compilation) of Ashtanga.
First chapter on fundamental principles (सूत्र स्थानम्/section of aphorisms).
Salutations to Lord Ganesha (गणेश/remover of obstacles).
Salutations to Lord Dhanvantari (धन्वन्तरि/god of medicine).
Salutations to Lord Sitaramachandra (सीतारामचंद्र/Lord Rama and Sita).
I bow (वंदे/salute) to the Lord of Lakshmi.
Salutations to the respected teachers (गुरुभ्यो/teachers).
Thirst is its long body, false imagination (विकल्प/doubts) is its head, hatred (प्रद्वेष/malice) is its vibrating hood, lust and anger (काम क्रोध/passion and wrath) are its poison, arguments (वितर्क/reasoning) are its teeth, and attachment (राग/desire) is its fierce eyes.
Ignorance (मोह/delusion) is its mouth, and it resides in the hollow (कोटर/cavity) of one's own body; to that Buddha who calmed this terrible mind-serpent (चित्त उरगं/snake of the mind) with the power of the mantra of wisdom (प्रज्ञा/intelligence), I bow down.
By whom all the innate (सहजाः/born together) diseases (रोगाः/illnesses) like attachment (राग/desire) and others, along with their root causes (समूला/with roots), were quickly destroyed from the entire universe (जगतः/world).
To that unique physician (वैद्यं/doctor), who knows the medical scriptures (वैद्य आगम ज्ञं/expert in medical texts), and to Brahma (पितामह/ancestor) and others, I bow my head.
Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्यायम्/section) on the desire for long life (आयुष्य कामीयम्/wishing for longevity).
Thus said the great sages (महर्षयः/great seers) Atreya and others.
By a person desiring long life (आयुः/lifespan) which is the means (साधनं/tool) to achieve righteousness (धर्म/duty), wealth (अर्थ/material prosperity), and happiness (सुख/pleasure).
The highest respect (परम आदरः/supreme regard) should be paid to the teachings of Ayurveda (आयुर्वेद उपदेशेषु/guidelines of ayurveda).
The eternal (सनातनं/ancient) and meaningful nectar of Ayurveda (आयुर्वेद अमृतं/elixir of life) was understood by Brahma (बुद्धा/awakened).
He gave it to Daksha, he gave it to the Ashvini twins (अश्विभ्यां/divine physicians), and they gave it to Indra (शतक्रतवे/performer of hundred sacrifices) after that.
By diseases (आमयैः/illnesses) which create obstacles (विघ्न कारिभिः/barrier creators) in the achievement of righteousness, wealth, desire, and liberation (मोक्ष/salvation).
When human beings (नरेषु/in people) were being afflicted (पीड्यमानेषु/suffering), keeping Punarvasu in the forefront.
Dhanvantari, Bharadvaja, Nimi, Kashyapa, and Kashyapa.
The great sages (महर्षयो/great seers) and high-souled ones, as well as Alambayana and others.
Approached Indra (शतक्रतम्/lord indra), the protector and king of the gods (अमरेश्वरं/lord of immortals).
Seeing them, the thousand-eyed one (सहस्राक्षो/indra) spoke exactly according to the scriptures (यथा आगमं/as per tradition).
The science that protects life (आयुषः पालकं/protector of lifespan), which is the secondary Veda (उपवेदम्/subsidiary veda) of Atharvaveda.
General medicine (काय/internal medicine), pediatrics (बाल/child health), demonology (ग्रह/psychiatry), ENT/ophthalmology (ऊर्ध्वांग/supra-clavicular branches), surgery (शल्य/surgical branch), toxicology (दंष्ट्रा/toxicology), rejuvenation (जरा/geriatrics), and aphrodisiacs (वृषैः/virility therapy).
Brahma (पितामहः/creator god) understood this sacred science which had attained eight branches (अष्टांगतां/eight-fold division).
Receiving that traditional knowledge (आम्नायं/sacred texts) and illuminating it among each other.
Wishing for a long and complete life (आयुः पुर्ण/full lifespan) for the world, the great sages (परमर्षयः/supreme seers) were delighted.
For the preservation (स्थिति अर्थम्/maintenance purpose) of Ayurveda, they then composed the treatises (तंत्राणि/textbooks).
Having made Agnivesha, Harita, Bhela, Mambya, Sushruta.
Karala and others as excellent disciples (सत् शिष्यान्/good students), the respected sages taught them.
Then they also composed their own respective treatises (तंत्रं/scientific texts), and after those were made.
The highly intelligent ones (सुमेधसः/wise people) narrated them to the teachers (गुरून्/masters) and the assembly of sages.
Praised by them, those treatises attained fame and stability (प्रतिष्ठां/recognition) on the earth.
Each single one of them was not comprehensive enough (अव्यापि/not pervasive) for the treatment of all diseases (समस्त ब्याधि/all illnesses).
But studying each treatise separately (प्रति तंत्र अभियोगे/dedication to each book) would lead to the destruction of a man's lifespan (पुरुष आयुष्य संक्षयः/loss of human life).
Because the same meaning (अर्थः/matter) has been stated repeatedly (पुनः पुनः/again and again) by study alone.
By the authors of treatises (तंत्र कारैः/text writers), the same matter is told somewhere with some specialty (विशेषतः/specific detail).
They were intent on conveying the meaning (अर्थ प्रत्यायन परा/focused on conveying meaning) and did not pay attention to the style of words.
Therefore, bringing together all the treatises (सर्व तंत्राणि/all textbooks), the Ashtanga Samgraha is composed.
Free from improper descriptions, excessive expansion (विस्तर/elaboration), objections, and repetitions (पुनरुक्त/reiteration).
Based only on the three pillars (स्कंध त्रय/three pillars) of causes (हेतु/etiology), signs/symptoms (लिंग/features), and therapeutics (औषध/medicine).
Illuminating the secret meanings and principles (तत्त्वानां/essentials) of various topics.
Largely resolving the contradictions (विरोधानां/conflicts) between one's own and other treatises.
Presented in accordance with the current era (युग अनुरूप/suited to the age), it will be done with proper division.
Not difficult to understand (अदुर्बोधं/easy to grasp) for daily use, pervading all branches, and brilliant.
Where internal medicine (काय चिकित्सितं/general therapeutics) has been summarized (संगृहीतं/compiled) with special emphasis.
Not even a tiny bit (मात्रा मात्रम्/even a small measure) here is devoid of scriptural authority (आगम वर्जितं/without scriptural backing).
Those subjects and that composition of text (ग्रंथ बंधः/textual structure) are arranged otherwise for brevity (संक्षेपाय/for conciseness).
Vata (वायुः/nervous system humor), Pitta, and Kapha are the three biological humors (दोषाः/vitiating factors) in brief.
Each of them is of three types based on increase (वृद्धि/aggregation), decrease (क्षय/diminution), and normal state (साम्य/equilibrium).
The increase and decrease are also of three types based on excellent, medium, and low degrees (उत्कृष्ट मध्य अल्पतया/high medium low).
In their abnormal (विकृत/vitiated) and normal (अविकृत/unvitiated) states, they destroy or sustain the body (देहं/physical frame).
Though they are all-pervading (व्यापिणः/pervasive), they reside below, in the middle, and above the heart and navel.
In relation to age, day, night, and digestion of food (भुक्तानां/eaten food), they correspond to the end, middle, and beginning respectively.
By them, the digestive fire (अग्निः/digestive fire) becomes irregular, sharp, and dull; and by their balanced state (समैः/equal), it becomes balanced.
The nature of the digestive tract (कोष्ठः/alimentary tract) becomes hard, soft, or medium; and it is medium even when they are balanced.
Present in the sperm and ovum (शुक्र आर्तव/male and female reproductive elements) at the time of birth, like poison in a poisonous insect.
By them, three constitutional types (प्रकृतयो/constitutions) are formed: low, medium, and excellent separately.
The constitution with balanced humors (सम धातुः/balanced tissues) is the best among all, and those born from two humors are blameworthy (निंद्या/censurable).
There, Vata (अनिलः/air humor) is dry (रुक्षो/unctuousless), light (लघुः/weightless), cold, rough (खरः/coarse), subtle, and moving.
Pitta is slightly unctuous (स स्नेह/with oiliness), sharp (तीक्ष्ण/penetrating), hot, light, foul-smelling, spreading (सरं/laxative), and liquid.
Kapha is unctuous (स्निग्धः/oily), cold, heavy (गुरुः/heavy), slow, smooth (श्लक्ष्णो/polished), slimy (मृत्स्नः/sticky), and stable.
Combination of two humors (संसर्गः/dual combination) and combination of all three humors (सन्निपातः/triple combination) happen due to their decrease and aggravation.
They are said to be of six types and ten types based on variations of predominance (उत्कर्ष/excel).
Plasma (रस/plasma), blood (असृक्/blood), muscle (मांस/muscle), fat (मेदः/adipose tissue), bone, marrow, and semen are the tissues (धातवः/sustaining tissues).
These seven are the structures capable of being vitiated (दूष्या/vitiable elements), and the wastes (मला/excreta) are urine, feces, sweat, etc.
The diseases (व्याधयः/illnesses) that arise when the humors reside in plasma and other tissues.
Are called born from them metaphorically (उपचारेण/by usage), like a ring of fire.
Nourishment (प्रीणनं/pleasing), life-sustenance (जीवनं/vitalizing), plastering, lubrication, supporting, and filling.
And production of embryo (गर्भ उत्पादः/conception) are known as the functions (कर्माणि/actions) of the tissues respectively.
These sustain the body (शरीरं/physique), and the diet of tissues (धातु आहारः/nourishment of tissues) happens always.
Increase of all elements is caused by similar things (समानैः/homologous factors), and the opposite happens by contrary things (विपरीतैः/heterologous factors).
The tastes (रसाः/tastes) are sweet (स्वादु/sweet), sour (अम्ल/acidic), salty, bitter (तिक्त/bitter), pungent (ऊषण/spicy), and astringent.
They reside in substances (द्रव्यम्/matter), and they bring strength (बल वहाः/strength carriers) in decreasing order.
There, the first three destroy Vata (मारुतं/wind humor), and the three starting with bitter destroy Kapha.
Astringent, bitter, and sweet pacify Pitta (पित्तम्/bile humor), while the others produce it.
Substance (द्रव्यम्/drug) is of three types: pacifying, aggravating, and beneficial for health (स्वस्थ हितं/good for healthy people).
Based on the excellence of hot and cold attributes (गुण/qualities), the potency (वीर्यं/potency) is remembered to be of two types.
The post-digestive taste (विपाको/metabolic transformation) of a substance is of three types: sweet, sour, and pungent.
Heavy, slow, cold (हिम/cold), unctuous, smooth, dense, soft, and stable.
These attributes along with subtle and clear are twenty (विंशतिः/twenty) in number, along with their opposites (विपर्ययाः/contraries).
Objects of senses, pervading, and liquefying (विकाषी/loosening) are other qualities.
Pervading substance (व्यवायी/diffusive) spreads through the whole body (देहम्/body) and then undergoes digestion.
Liquefying substance (विकाषी/depressing) loosens the joints and bonds (संधि बंधान्/joints and ligaments) even while spreading.
Sharp and intense are also conceptualized by some sages.
Sattva (सत्वं/purity), Rajas (रजः/passion), and Tamas (तमः/darkness) are stated to be the three great mental qualities (महागुणाः/great attributes).
The combination of time, sense objects, and action (कर्मणां/actions) which is deficient, perverse, or excessive.
And proper combination (सम्यक् योगः/right utilization) should be known as the sole cause of disease and health (आरोग्य/wellness).
Disease (रोगः/illness) is the inequality of humors (दोष वैषम्यं/humoral imbalance), and health is the equilibrium of humors.
Diseases are also considered to be of two types based on the division of endogenous (निज/internal) and exogenous (आगंतु/accidental).
Their location (अधिष्ठानम्/seat) is also of two types based on the division of body and mind (मनो/mind).
Rajas and Tamas are declared as the two humors of the mind (मनसो/of the mind).
One should examine the patient (रोगिणं/sick person) through inspection (दर्शन/visual examination), palpation (स्पर्शन/touching), and interrogation.
And examine the disease through etiology (निदान/cause), prodromal symptoms (प्राग्रूप/premonitory signs), clinical features (लक्षण/symptoms), therapeutic suitability, and pathogenesis.
Based on the division of land and body, habitat (देशम्/region) is said to be of two types here.
Arid land (जांगलं/desert forest land) is predominant in Vata, and marshy land (अनूपं/wet land) is dominant in Kapha.
The ordinary land (साधारणं/normal land) has balanced humors; thus one should indicate the land region in three types.
Time measured by moments, etc., and the stage of disease (व्याधि अवस्था/clinical stage) is the coordinator of medicine.
Purification (शोधनं/cleansing) and pacification (शमनं/palliation)—thus in brief, medicine (औषधं/drug) is of two types.
For the humors born in the body (शरीर जानां/somatic), the supreme medicine (परम औषधं/best remedy) in order.
Is enema (बस्ती/medicated enema), purgation (विरेको/therapeutic purgation), emesis (वमनं/therapeutic vomiting), and oil, ghee, and honey.
Intellect (धी/wisdom), courage (धैर्य/fortitude), and knowledge of the soul are the supreme medicine for mental disorders.
After this, the summary of chapters (अध्याय संग्रहः/compilation of chapters) of this treatise will be stated.
Desire for long life, welfare of the disciple (शिष्य अर्थ/for student's benefit), daily routine, seasonal routine, and non-origin of diseases.
Knowledge of liquids, knowledge of solid food, protection of life, forbidden foods, and knowledge of eating and drinking.
Quantity of food, protection of eyes, knowledge of medicines, excellence of purification, etc.
Great decoctions, various substances, and the division of tastes.
Knowledge of humors, etc., that medical knowledge, their actions, diseases, and medicines.
Therapeutics, oleation (स्नेह/lubrication therapy), sudation (स्वेद/sweating therapy), purification, non-unctuous enema, and nasal medication.
Smoking therapy, gargling, ear filling, application of pastes, instruments (यंत्र/appliances), and leeches (जलौकसः/leeches).
Method of bloodletting (सिरा विधिः/venesection), method of foreign body removal, surgical procedures, alkali therapy, and cauterization.
These forty chapters (चत्वारिंशत्/forty) are called the section on fundamental principles (सूत्रं/aphorism section) and anatomy/physiology.
Desire for a son, descent of embryo (गर्भ अवक्रांति/embryology), regimen during pregnancy, management of complications, and anatomy.
Veins, vital spots (मर्म/vulnerable points), description of constitution, deformities of organs, and fatal signs of diseases.
Along with the messenger, these twelve chapters (द्वादश अध्याया/twelve chapters) are on the diagnosis of all diseases.
Fever (ज्वर/fever), bleeding disorders, dyspnea, consumption, intoxication, hemorrhoids (अर्शः/piles), and diarrhea.
Urinary retention, diabetes (प्रमेहाणां/urinary disorders), abscess, and diseases of the abdomen.
Anemia (पांढु/pallor), skin diseases (कुष्ठ/dermatological conditions), those afflicted by Vata, and gout—these sixteen chapters.
And treatment of fever, bleeding disorders, cough, dyspnea, consumption.
Vomiting, alcoholism, hemorrhoids, and two chapters each for urinary disorders.
Abscess, abdominal tumors (गुल्म/phantom tumor), abdominal diseases, anemia, swelling, and erysipelas.
Skin diseases, vitiligo (श्वित्र/leukoderma), Vata diseases, and treatment of gout.
Twenty-four chapters (चर्तुर्विंशतिः/twenty-four); after that is the section on pharmaceutics and success in treatment.
Pharmaceutics of emesis, of purgation, its success, and formulation of enema.
Success and formulation of enemas, and success of enemas and unctuous enemas.
Formulation of substances is the eighth, and the next section is the Uttaratantra (उत्तरं/subsequent section).
Care of children (बाल उपचरणे/pediatric care), diseases of children, knowledge of planetary afflictions, and their prevention.
Bathing rituals, specific seizures, demonic possession, insanity (उन्मादे/psychosis), and loss of memory.
Diseases of eyelids and joints—two chapters each, vision disorders, and blindness (लिंग नाशिषु/cataract).
All diseases of vision, throbbing of eyes, inflammation of eyes, and diseases of ears, nose, and mouth.
Diseases of the head, wounds (व्रणे/ulcers)—likewise two chapters each, immediate fractures (भंगे/fractures), and fistula-in-ano.
Tumors, etc., minor diseases (क्षुद्र रोगेषु/minor ailments), and diseases of genitals—two chapters separately.
In poisons, two chapters on snakes and insects, and two chapters on spiders and rats.
In poison, use of poison, and likewise the chapter on rejuvenation (रसायने/anti-aging therapy).
Aiming at aphrodisiac therapy (वाजीकरणम्/virility therapy), the fiftieth chapter completes the branches.
One hundred and fifty chapters (पञ्चाशद् अध्याय शतं/one hundred fifty chapters) are spoken in six sections (स्थानैः/divisions).
Thus ends the first chapter (प्रथमो अध्यायः/first chapter) named Ayushkamiya.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on the initiation of a student (शिष्य उपनयनीयम्/student's induction).
The teacher (गुरु/instructor) said that the student should be endowed with intellect (धी/intelligence), memory (स्मृति/retention), and skill (पाटव/proficiency).
He should have straight mouth, nose, and eyes, and a thin, glossy, and pleasing complexion (त्वक् छवि/skin texture).
He should be a celibate (ब्रह्मचारी/student observing celibacy), conquering dualities (जित द्वंद्वः/unaffected by joy or sorrow), courageous (धीरः/patient), of good conduct (सुचरितः/well-behaved), and stable (स्थिरः/steady).
He should have stayed with the teacher for six months, be capable (शक्तः/competent), and endowed with modesty (लज्जा/shame) and cleanliness (शौच/purity).
Such a student (शिष्य/disciple) is fit to be taught until the end (अंतम्/completion) of the treatise, its meaning, and its practical application (कर्मणाम्/clinical procedures).
One should not study during untimely lightning and thunder, earthquakes (भू कंपे/seismic movements), or during the visibility of Rahu (राहु दर्शने/solar or lunar eclipse).
One should also not study on the fifteenth day of the lunar cycle (पंचदश्याम्/new moon day) when the moon is absent, or in the absence (परोक्षे/behind the back) of the teacher.
He should not read without splitting words (अविच्छिन्न पदम्/without pausing between words), not too slow (मंदम्/sluggish), and not in too high or too low pitch (नीचकैः/low tone).
He should serve (पर्युपासीत/worship or attend upon) the benevolent and supreme teacher (आचार्यम्/preceptor) like a king (राजवत्/royally).
He should sleep only after the teacher has slept, and wake up before him (पूर्वतः/earlier).
He should not address the teacher by name alone, nor should he make fun of him even in jest (विनोदयेत्/amusement).
He should be loyal (अभेद्यः/trustworthy), humble (अनुद्धतः/not arrogant), gentle, truthful (सूनृतः/speaking pleasant truth), and pleasant to look at (प्रिय दर्शनः/handsome).
He should be highly learned (बहु श्रुतः/knowledgeable), knocked in time (काल वेदी/knowing the right time), well-versed in texts, and knowing the meaning of treatises (अर्थ शास्त्र वित्/expert in science).
He should treat the helpless (अनाथान्/destitute) and the sick (रोगिणः/patients) just like his own son (पुत्रवत्/filial care).
Permitted and blessed by the teacher, he obtains the title (शब्दम्/designation) of a physician (भिषक्/doctor).
He who only knows the theoretical science (शास्त्र ज्ञः/bookish knowledge) but is unaccomplished in clinical work (कर्मसु अपरिनिष्ठितः/unskilled in practical application).
He becomes confused (मुह्यति/deluded) upon reaching a patient (आतुरम्/sick person), just like a coward (भीरुः/frightened person) upon reaching a battlefield (आहवम्/war).
On the other hand, he who performs medical treatments out of audacity (धार्ष्ट्यात्/foolhardiness) without following the scriptures (शास्त्र विविवर्जितः/devoid of textual guidance).
He incurs the blame (गर्हाम्/censure) of good people and invites punishment (वधम्/execution or severe penalty) from the king (राजतः/government).
He who has fourfold knowledge (ज्ञानम् चतुर्विधम्/comprehensive understanding) regarding causes (हेतौ/etiology), signs (लिंगे/symptoms), cure, and non-recurrence (अपुनर्भवे/prevention of relapse) of diseases.
Such a supreme physician (भिषक् तमः/best doctor) is truly fit for kings (राजार्हः/worthy of royalty).
Weapons, scriptures, and water depend on the quality or defect (गुण दोष/virtue or vice) of the person utilizing them.
Depending on the recipient or vessel (पात्र आश्रय/deserving container), hence one should expand his wisdom (प्रज्ञाम्/intelligence) through extensive listening (बाहु श्रुत्येन/constant study).
The treatise (शास्त्रम्/medical textbook) is like a lamp (प्रदीप भूतम्/guiding light), and a broad mind (विपुला मतिः/vast intellect) is like vision.
Therefore, a physician treating patients with both of these well-utilized attributes never commits a mistake (अपराध्यति/fails or errs).
A physician who goes immediately when called (आहूतः/summoned), well-dressed (सुवेषः/decently attired), and observing good omens (सुनिमित्ततः/auspicious signs).
And having gone there, does not fix his mind anywhere else except on the well-being of the patient (आतुर अर्थत्/patient's welfare).
He examines the diseases properly, especially through the two causes (द्विदानादि/primary diagnostic factors) and other specific methods.
And he does not disclose or broadcast outside the confidential information (गोपनीयाम् वार्त्ताम्/secret matters) of that patient.
And he does not suddenly skip or delay the appropriate time of treatment (क्रिया कालम्/therapeutic window) even for that patient.
He who knows how to attend to and care for the patient, that physician (वैद्याः/doctor) attains success (सिद्धिम्/accomplishment).
He should not accept any meat or gift (आमिषम्/gratification) from women when their guardian or husband is absent (पराङ्मुखे/averse or away).
He should avoid staying alone in private places (रहसि स्थानम्/solitude) and joking or laughing (परिहाशम्/frivolity) with women.
The physician should avoid treating a patient who is hated by the king or good doctors, or who hates them, or who is an enemy (द्विषम्/adversary).
Or who is fierce (चंडम्/furious), overwhelmed with grief (शोक आतुरम्/sorrowful), terrified (भीरुम्/cowardly), ungrateful (कृतघ्नम्/thankless), or thinks himself to be a doctor (वैद्य मानिनम्/arrogant about medical knowledge).
Or who lacks necessary equipment (हीन उपकरणम्/devoid of medicines/tools), is highly anxious (व्यग्रम्/distracted), disobedient (अविधेयम्/unmanageable), or whose lifespan is exhausted (गत आयुषम्/nearing death).
A patient desiring to live (जिजीविषुः/wishing to survive), even though afflicted with disease, should avoid doctors devoid of the previously mentioned good qualities.
Such doctors sell their medical treatments (क्रिया विक्रयिणः/commercializing healthcare) and are truly the precursors (अग्रेसराः/forerunners) of death (मृत्योः/destruction).
The physician (भिषक्/doctor), the substances (द्रव्याणि/medicines), the attendant (उपस्थाता/nurse), and the patient (रोगी/sick person) form the four pillars (पाद चतुष्टयम्/four components of treatment).
Each of these components is specified to possess four essential qualities (चतुर् गुणम्/quadruple virtues) for successful treatment (चिकित्सितस्य/therapeutics).
The physician should be skillful (दक्षः/expert), have clear knowledge of textual meanings obtained from an authoritative source (तीर्त्थात्/from a teacher), have seen practical work (दृष्ट कर्मा/experienced), and be pure (शुचिः/clean).
The medicine (औषधम्/drug) should be capable of multiple formulations (बहु कल्पम्/various dosage forms), have many virtues (बहु गुणम्/rich in attributes), be fully endowed with potency (संपन्नम्/mature), and be appropriate (योग्यम्/suitable).
The attendant or nurse (परिचारकः/caregiver) should be affectionate (अनुरक्तः/devoted), pure (शुचिः/hygienic), skillful (दक्षः/efficient), and intelligent (बुद्धिमान्/wise).
The patient should be wealthy (आढ्यः/afford treatment), compliant to the physician (भिषक् वश्यः/obedient), capable of expressing his symptoms (ज्ञापकः/expressive), and full of mental strength (सत्त्ववान्/courageous).
If the physician is devoid of good qualities (विगुणे/unqualified), the other components, even if endowed with good virtues, become useless (अनर्थकाः/fruitless).
Whereas a virtuous physician (गुणवान्/qualified doctor) can manage and palliate (यापयेत्/prolong life) even those patients whose other three components are deficient (पाद हीनान्/lacking other pillars).
Therefore, wise men know that the physician alone is the primary cause (प्रधानम् कारणम्/main factor) of treatment (चिकित्सायाः/medical cure).
Diseases are of two types: curable (साध्यः/remediable) and incurable (असाध्यः/incurable); and those are again subdivided into two types each.
They are easily curable (सुसाध्यः/easy to cure), curable with difficulty (कृच्छ्र साध्यः/challenging to cure), palliable (याप्यः/manageable but incurable), and completely untreatable (अनुपक्रमः/beyond therapy).
A disease is easily curable in a body capable of tolerating all medicines (सर्व औषध क्षमे/strong constitution), in a young man (यूनः/youthful person), and in one who has self-control (जित आत्मनः/disciplined mind).
It should not affect vital spots (अमर्मगः/not involving vital organs), have mild causes (अल्प हेतु/minor etiology), mild primary signs, mild symptoms, and be without complications (अनुपद्रवः/free from secondary ailments).
The vitiated humor, habitat, season, and constitution should not be identical (अतुल्य/dissimilar), and the four pillars of treatment should be fully endowed with wealth/qualities (पाद संपदि/prosperity of components).
When the planetary influences are favorable (अनुगुणेषु/friendly), involving only one humor or pathway (एक दोष मार्गः/single path), new or acute (नवः/recent), then the disease is comfortable (सुखः/easy).
An easily curable disease (सुख साध्यः/readily remediable) requires simple measures (सुख उपायः/easy remedies) and is cured in a short period of time (कालेन अल्पेन/quickly).
On the other hand, a disease curable with difficulty (कृच्छ्रः/arduous) requires difficult and major measures (महद्भिः उपायैः/extensive treatments) and takes a long time (चिरेण/prolonged duration) to heal.
An incurable disease is mixed with terminal signs (असाध्य लिंग संकीर्णः/confounded with fatal symptoms) and may require surgical interventions (शस्त्र आदि साधनः/operative procedures) and the like.
A palliable disease (याप्यः/manageable) is sustained due to the remnant lifespan (शेषत्वात् आयुषः/remaining longevity) by using wholesome diet and lifestyle (पथ्यैः/wholesome regimens), usually opposite to the causing factors.
It spreads or flares up (प्रतन्यते/aggravates) even by a slight cause (अल्पेन हेतुना/minor trigger) after giving temporary relief (अल्पम् सुखम्/slight comfort).
The disease does not completely disappear or go to zero (न अशेषताम्/never gets fully eradicated) because it is born of past actions (कर्मजः/karma-born) and bound by fixed lifespan (नियत आयुषः/destined life).
Just as a falling building is supported and held up by wooden pillars or props (विष्कंभैः/supports), similarly the patient (आतुरः/sick person) is maintained here by wholesome regimes (हितैः/beneficial things).
The other extreme variety is completely incurable, defies all treatments (सर्वाः क्रियाः/all therapeutic actions), and transcends or goes beyond all hope of recovery (प्रत्याख्येयः/to be rejected).
Therefore, this patient should be neglected or refused treatment (उपेक्ष्यः/abandoned), as he stands in complete opposition (अत्यंत विपर्यये/extreme contrariness) to signs of life.
It causes giddiness (भ्रम/vertigo), delusion (मोह/unconsciousness), restlessness (अरति/disquiet), exhibits fatal signs (दृष्ट रिष्टः/manifesting pre-fatal symptoms), and destroys the sense organs (अक्ष नाशनः/loss of senses).
A physician should first examine the disease thoroughly in this manner, and only then start the treatment or clinical action (क्रियाम्/therapy).
Otherwise, he will certainly attain loss of self-interest, loss of knowledge, and loss of reputation (यशो हानिम्/damage to fame).
Even when two curable diseases combine together in a weak person (अबलिनः/devoid of strength), the condition becomes incurable (असाध्यताम्/state of incurability).
Therefore, one should know that the combination of a curable and an incurable disease (साध्य असाध्य समागमम्/conjunction of remediable and irremediable) results only in incurability.
An incurable disease never becomes curable, but a curable disease can turn into an incurable one.
Due to improper management of the four pillars (पाद अपचारात्/mismanagement of components) or due to fate (दैवात्/providence), diseases change their state into a worse stage (अवस्थान्तरम्/another pathological state).
It is better to handle the venom of a venomous snake (आशीविष विषम्/serpent venom) or a blazing fire (दीप्तम् अग्निम्/burning fire).
But a wise physician should never accept monetary gains or meat (आमिषम्/temptation) from a suffering patient through deceptive appearances (रूपेण अञ्जनात्/pretentious behavior).
The best of physicians (भिषक् वरः/excellent doctor) treats suffering patients out of righteousness (धर्मः/duty), considering them as unseen divine beings (परोक्ष भूत/invisible entities).
He who practices medicine with this attitude achieves his life goals (सिद्धार्थः/successful) and transcends or excels over everything (सर्वम् अतिवर्त्तते/surpasses all boundaries).
Thus ends the second chapter (द्वितीयः अध्यायः/second section) named 'The Initiation of a Student' (शिष्य उपनयनीयः/student's induction chapter).




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter on daily routine (दिनचर्या/daily lifestyle regimen).
One should wake up during the auspicious period before dawn (ब्राह्ममुहूर्त/early morning time) while reflecting on the proper digestion or indigestion (जीर्ण-अजीर्ण/state of food digestion) of the previous meal.
To protect life (आयुषः/span of life), a healthy person (स्वस्थो/fit individual) should respond to the natural urges (जात-वेगः/calls of nature) and eliminate waste.
One should face north (उदङ्मुखो/facing northwards) during the day and face south (दक्षिणामुहूर्त/facing southwards) at night while passing urine and stool (मूत्र-शकृत्/excrements).
Controlling speech (वाचं नियम्य/maintaining silence), being humble, covering the body, and wrapping the head (अवगुंठितः/veiled or covered).
One should allow the naturally initiated urge to flow out, but should never force (उदीरयेत्/strain or artificially induce) it.
One should not eliminate waste on unholy paths, soil, ash, cow sheds, or places covered with cow dung (गोमये/bovine manure).
In ancient ruins, near habitations, on anthills (वल्मीक/termite mounds), beautiful places, fertile lands, cremation grounds (चिति/funeral pyre areas), or under trees.
Nor facing towards women, worshipful entities (पूज्य/venerable individuals), cows, the sun, the moon, wind, fire, or water.
Nor by disrespecting the earth, except under conditions of fear or physical inability (भय-अशक्त्योः/helplessness due to terror or weakness), but never by deliberate desire.
One under the influence of natural urges should not engage in other work, nor ignore curable diseases (साध्यम् आमयं/treatable illnesses) without conquering them.
Cleanse the anal and urethral paths (मल-अयनः/excreta passages) using clean, stone-free clods of earth (मृत्-पिंडी/clay balls).
One should use water fetched from a clean source and pure earth (मृद्भिः/soil) for washing.
The purification (शौचम्/cleanness) should completely remove the paste and odor without letting the water droplets splash back.
After touching the tissues (धातून/body elements), waste products, tears, eyes, hair, or clipped nails.
After bathing, before intending to eat, after eating, after sleeping, after sneezing, and before worshipping the deities (सुरा-अर्चने/divine worship).
Or after walking on the main road (रथ्याम्/street), one should sit facing north and rinse the mouth (आचामेद्/perform sipping of water for purification).
Or facing east, sitting in a secluded place (विविक्तस्थो/lonely spot), not placing the hands outside the knees, and not looking elsewhere.
Without talking, wearing an upper garment (उत्तरासंगी/shoulder cloth), using clean water passing over the root of the thumb.
The water should not be lifted too high, the body should not be bent too much or held too high, and the water should not be boiled directly on fire or foul-smelling (पूतिभिः/putrid).
Water with froth, bubbles, or alkaline properties (क्षारैः/chemicals) should not be used, nor should it be poured with only one hand.
Not with one wet hand, not with unclean hands or feet (अमेध्य-हस्त-पादो/dirty extremities), and without making any sound.
The twig for brushing teeth should be selected from banyan (वट/ficus), keehar, sun, khadir (खदिर/acacia), karanj, or oleander trees.
Or originating from arjuna, kachnar, apamarg, maruti, or kadamba trees.
The twig should possess astringent, bitter, or pungent (कषाय-तिक्त-कटुकं/taste properties) tastes, or other roots of similar nature.
The taste should be well-known, the tip should be chewed into a brush (क्षुण्ण-अग्रम्/crushed top), it should be straight, without knots, and grown in fertile soil.
Its thickness should equal the tip of the little finger (कनीनी-अग्र/smallest finger size) and it should be prepared well to twelve fingers (द्वादश-अङ्गुलं/standard measurement unit) in length.
Eating early in the morning and maintaining silence (यत-वाक्/restrained speech), one should brush the teeth using this toothbrushing stick (दंत-धावनं/herbal tooth-twig).
One should rub the teeth with a paste made of trikatu, trijataka, and honey (क्षौद्र/madhu).
Gently rub the teeth and the gums (दंत-मांसानि/gingival tissues) with that twig without causing any injury or pain.
One should scrape the tongue comfortably from back to front using a tongue scraper (जिह्वा-निर्लेखनेन/tongue cleaner).
By doing this, the accumulation of dirt, bad taste, foul smell of the tongue, mouth, and teeth (दंत-जाः/dental origins) are eliminated.
Appetite, clarity, and lightness (रुचि-वैशद्य-लघुता/taste appreciation, cleanness, and weightlessness) of the mouth are attained.
This should not be used by individuals suffering from indigestion, vomiting (वमथु/emesis), asthma, cough, fever, or facial paralysis (अर्दिती/facial palsy).
Nor by those suffering from morbid thirst, mouth ulcers, heart diseases, eye disorders, headaches, or ear aches (कर्ण-आमयी/otic illnesses).
Never use twigs from shleshemataka, neem (अरिष्ट/margosa), baheda, dhav, or dhanvana trees.
Or bilva, babhul, nirgundi, sahjan (शिग्रु/drumstick), tilvak, or tinduka trees.
Or kachnar, shami, pilu, peepal, ingudi, or guggulu trees.
Or amaltas, tamarind (अम्लीका/sour herb), banana, shalmal tree, or sunn hemp plants.
Twigs that are sweet, sour, salty, dry, hollow (सुषिरं/perforated), foul-smelling, or slimy should be avoided.
Toothbrushing sticks, seats, and footwear made from palash orasan wood should be discarded (त्यजेत्/abandoned).
First brush the lower teeth, then wash the eyes (लोचने/vision organs) in the morning.
Fill the mouth with water and sprinkle the eyes with cold water (शीत-वारिणा/chilly liquid) during summer and autumn seasons.
After bowing down to the gods and elders (वृद्धान्/senior citizens), listen to hundreds of auspicious and beneficial words.
After listening, one should immediately look at ghee (सर्पिः/clarified butter) kept in a gold vessel.
Thereafter, one should regularly apply Sauveera ointment (अंजनं/collyrium) which is highly beneficial for the eyes.
By applying it, the eyes become beautiful, capable of microscopic vision (सूक्ष्म-दर्शने/minute observation).
With distinct three colors, clear, and adorned with beautiful, thick eyelashes (घन-पक्ष्मणी/dense lashes).
The eye is full of light (तेजोमयं/luminous nature), and it faces a specific danger from kapha humor (श्लेष्मतो भयं/phlegm risk).
Therefore, once in every seven nights, extract collyrium (रस-अंजनं/herbal extract drops) should be applied to induce watering of the eyes.
Thereafter, execute nasal drops (नस्यं/nasal therapy) using Anu oil, followed by holding a mouth rinse (गंडूष-धारणं/fluid retention in mouth).
This practice grants firm, elevated, and beautiful skin, shoulders, neck, face, and chest (वक्षसः/thoracic region).
A fragrant mouth, pleasant voice (निःस्वना/sound or vocal resonance), and clear, sharp sense organs are achieved.
Those who regularly practice nasal therapy (नस्य-शिलिनः/nasal drops users) will never get wrinkles, grey hair, or facial dark spots.
Cracking of lips, roughness, dryness of mouth, and tooth disorders (द्विजामयाः/dental ailments).
And voice loss or hoarseness will never occur due to holding oil rinses (स्नेह-गंडूष/unctuous gargle).
Holding a large mouthful of decoction (कवल-ग्रहः/gargle fluid) made from khadir, milky trees, or arimed water.
Cures anorexia, bad taste in the mouth, dirt, foul smell, and excessive salivation (प्रसेक-जित्/saliva control).
By holding comfortably warm water rinses, lightness of the mouth (वक्त्र-लाघवं/oral lightness) is quickly generated.
Thereafter, one should practice daily herbal smoking (धूमं/medicinal smoking) and wear scents and flower garlands.
Due to medicinal smoking, diseases of the supraclavicular region (ऊर्ध्व-जत्रूत्था/above neck zone) caused by vata and kapha will not occur.
The kapha humor aggravated by collyrium is eliminated by nasal drops, and that brought into the throat by nasal therapy is expelled by gargling.
The kapha humors brought into the mouth by gargling are conquered in sequence by smoking; wearing scents and garlands acts as an aphrodisiac, removes misfortune, and serves as an embellishment (प्रसाधनं/beautification).
One should not wear worn-out, dirty, blood-stained, or gaudy (उल्बणं/excessive or flashy) clothes.
Garlands should not be excessively long, worn outside inappropriately, or contain red flowers, except those born in water (जल-जाद्/aquatic origins like lotus).
Never wear clothes, flowers, or footwear (उपानहौ/shoes) that have been worn by others.
Desiring taste, clarity, and fragrance, one should hold beneficial herbs in the mouth (वक्त्रेण/by oral cavity).
Along with jasmine, clove (लवङ्ग/spices), camphor, kankola, and mace.
Chewing the tender leaf of betel plant (तांबूलीनां/betel leaves) combined with heart-pleasing betel nut is beneficial.
It is unwholesome for those suffering from bleeding disorders (रक्तपित्त/bleeding diathesis), chest injury, emaciation, dryness, and inflamed eyes (उत्कुपित-चक्षुषां/irritated eyes).
It is unwholesome (अपथ्यं/unhealthy regimen) for those suffering from poisoning, fainting, intoxication, and phthisis (शोषिणां/emaciation).
It is wholesome (पथ्यं/salutary routine) for a person after waking up from sleep, after eating, after bathing, or after vomiting.
Two betel leaves, one betel nut (पूगं/areca nut), along with a little slaked lime and khadir should be consumed.
Then one should rise to earn wealth through means that are bound with righteous purposes (अर्थ-अनुबंधिषु/productive and ethical goals).
A long life span is condemned if it lacks the proper means of livelihood (साधनं/resources or assets).
Agriculture, trade, cattle rearing (गो-रक्षाम्/bovine protection), or serving a virtuous king through proper strategies.
A person desirous of wealth (धन-अर्थी/money seeker) should resort to actions that are not contradictory to both worlds (earthly and heavenly).
After relieving the natural urges, one should clean the passages, take food, and maintain balance with women.
Holding auspicious items with the hand, one should step out after worshipping gods, ghee, and auspicious objects (मङ्गलं/benign symbols).
One should walk with an umbrella and footwear (पदत्राणो/shoes), looking only a yoke's length ahead on the ground.
At night, or when an urgent task arises, one must walk holding a staff, wearing a turban, and accompanied by a companion (सहायवान्/partner or helper).
One should wander covering the body at night, but never cover the head during the day (अहनि/daytime).
Sacred trees, worshipful entities, flags, forbidden shadows, ash, husks, and unclean things (अशुचीन्/polluted objects).
One should not step over them, nor walk over gravel, clods of earth, ritual offerings, or bathing places.
At midday, during evening twilights (संध्यायोः/junction moments of day), at night, or at midnight, one should avoid cross roads.
One should not reside or stay under trees near sacred places or at public squares during the night (शर्वर्य्यां/nighttime).
Slaughterhouses, forests (अटवी/woods), abandoned houses, and cremation grounds should be avoided even during the day.
One should not make arrogant sounds or mock auspicious entities, worshipful gods, or benign items (मङ्गलानि/good omens).
One should not circumambulate holy places in an anti-clockwise manner, nor walk clockwise around unholy things.
One should bow down to crossroads (चतुष्पथं/four-way junctions) and known great trees.
One should not travel using animals that are vicious, diseased, unholy, untrained, hungry, or thirsty.
Nor with animals having docked tails, or those blind in one eye (मैकाक्षैः/one-eyed), and riding on the back of a cow should be avoided.
One should not travel in highly muddy areas, very late in the evening, or when the sun is at the center of the sky (नभो-मध्य-गे/zenith position).
Not without keeping water close by, not too fast (अति-तूर्णं/excessively rapid), and not continuously without breaks.
Not with enemies, not with unknown people (अविदितैः/unfamiliar persons), not alone, and not with unrighteous companions.
One should give way to travelers, the distressed, the elderly, women, those carrying weights, traders, and twice-born brahmins (द्विजन्मने/priests or scholars).
Bathing, eating, and drinking should not be done before offering them to outsiders first.
One should not cross a river using only arms, nor walk directly facing a heavy storm or dust ridge (अभि-स्कंधम्/frontal assault of wind/dust).
One should not climb a dangerous mountain, a doubtful boat, or an unstable tree (तरुं/plant or tree).
One should not knock down a fruit from a tree using a clod of earth or another fruit.
One should not enter a place when forbidden, nor through an improper entrance (अद्वारेण/wrong opening), nor sit on an improper seat.
One should stand on their own in another's house, and should not awaken a person who is enjoying proper sleep (युक्त-निद्रं/sound slumber).
One should not indulge in fickle movements of hands, speech, feet, sticks, eyes, or abdomen (उदर-चापलं/abdominal instability).
Thrice in a fortnight (पक्षस्य/fifteen days duration), one should get their hair, mustache, nails, and body hair trimmed.
One should not clip them with their own hands or teeth, and should perform bathing regularly.
Now, when the desire for food and drink arises, one should perform oil massage using vata-alleviating, fragrant oils.
The oil massage should be pleasant to touch according to the season (यथा-ऋतु/as per seasonal changes).
Oil massage destroys vata, provides nourishment (पुष्टि/growth), benefits skin, and cures exhaustion, stiffness, and gives strength.
It dispels burns, fractures, injuries, pain, fatigue (क्लम/mental fatigue), physical exhaustion, and aging.
Just like the axle of a chariot, leather, or a pot become strong, similar benefits occur to the body through oil massage (अभ्यङ्गतो/by unction therapy).
The sense of touch is dominated by vata, and touch resides in the skin (त्वक्-आश्रयं/located in skin fabric).
And oil massage is highly beneficial for the skin, which is why one should practice it regularly.
One should practice it specifically on the head, ears, and feet (पादेषु/on lower extremities).
When practiced on the head, it promotes hair growth (केश्यः/beneficial for hair), nourishes the skull, and satisfies the sense organs.
Filling the ears with oil cures pain of the jaw, neck, head, and earache (कर्ण-शूल/ear pain).
Foot massage grants stability to the feet, promotes sleep, and clarifies the vision (दृक्-प्रसाद-कृत्/eyesight clarity provider).
It cures numbness of feet, fatigue, stiffness, contractions, and cracking of feet (स्फुटन/fissures).
Oil massage is forbidden (व्रज्र्यो/contraindicated) for those afflicted with kapha, those who have just undergone purification therapies, and those with indigestion.
Physical action that generates bodily tiredness is called exercise (व्यायामः/physical workout).
Lightness, capability to perform work, stimulated digestive fire (दीप्तो अग्निः/heightened appetite), and depletion of excess fat are achieved.
A well-defined, firm body structure (घन-गात्रत्वं/compact body build) is born out of exercise.
Persons suffering from vata and pittadi diseases, children, the elderly, and those with indigestion should abandon it.
It should be practiced up to half of one's total strength (अर्द्ध-शक्त्या/half capacity) by strong individuals who consume unctuous food.
During winter and spring seasons, it should be done up to half-strength, but much less (मन्दम्/mildly) during other seasons.
After performing it, one should gently massage the entire body comfortably (समंततः/all over).
Thirst, emaciation, severe asthma (प्रतमको/deep dyspnea), bleeding disorders, exhaustion, and mental fatigue.
Cough, fever, and vomiting are produced due to excessive exercise (अति-व्यायामतः/over-exercising).
Over-indulgence in exercise, staying awake at night, walking long distances, sexual intercourse, excessive laughing, and talking is dangerous.
A person who indulges excessively in these acts perishes just like a lion perishing while trying to drag an elephant (गजं/tusker).
Powder massage (उद्धर्त्तनं/dry herbal powder rubbing) destroys kapha and liquefies the excess fat.
It grants firmness to the body parts and serves as an excellent skin-clarifying agent (त्वक्-प्रसाद-करं/skin complexion enhancer).
Bathing stimulates digestion, acts as an aphrodisiac, promotes longevity (आयुष्यं/long life), and bestows vitality and strength.
It conquers itching, dirt, exhaustion, sweat, drowsiness, thirst, burning sensation, and sins (पाप्म-जित्/evil effects).
Pouring warm water over the lower body brings strength (बल-आवहः/strength provider).
But pouring the same warm water over the head robs the strength of the hair and eyes.
One should not bathe without immersing the head, nor bathe in scanty or freezing cold water (शीतले/chilled liquid).
One should not step into bathing water while sweating or when the body is overheated.
One should not bathe in another's water body without first lifting five lumps of clay to clean it.
One should not look at one's reflection in water, nor stand on the edge of a dangerous water reservoir (जला-शयं/water body).
One should not splash or beat the water using hands or feet.
After bathing, do not wipe the body parts harshly, and do not vigorously shake the hair (शिरोरुहान्/head hair).
One should not immediately wear wet clothes, or a turban, but wear clean, washed garments.
Do not touch or wear clothes previously worn by others, or clothes stained with oil or grease.
Use separate clothes for sleeping, separate for going out, and separate for worshipping the deities (देवता-अर्चने/divine rituals).
Bathing is contraindicated (गर्हितं/forbidden) for those suffering from facial paralysis, eye diseases, mouth diseases, ear diseases, and diarrhea.
And for those suffering from flatulence, chronic coryza, indigestion, and immediately after taking food.
One should eat food according to the rules of dietetics (अन्न-पान-विधानेन/dietary regulations) without excessive greed or delay.
Rejoicing with a pleasant mind, offering oblations to fire, and giving donations according to one's capacity.
The cooked food should be consumed with water, in a secluded place, comfortably, and without criticizing it.
One should offer food dedicating it to all living beings, and should not cook food solely for oneself (आत्मने/for own self).
One should not eat food belonging to those facing death, the dead, or those living a miserable life.
Nor food from women who dominate their husbands, impotent persons, outcasts, or cruel evildoers.
Nor food from groups, enemies, prostitutes, public feasts, or cheats.
One should not eat snacks kept on the lap, nor drink water directly using the palms (अंजलिना/folded palms cup).
All items mixed with sesame seeds (तिल-सम्बद्धं/sesame preparations) should not be eaten after sunset.
Do not exert physically immediately after eating, and do not indulge in forbidden pleasures.
Spend the middle part of the day engaged in righteous and meaningful stories comprising the three goals of life.
One should pass the midday accompanied by wise and disciplined companions (सहायवान्/helpers or friends).
One should not associate with people hated by the public or the king, nor with atheists (नास्तिकैः/non-believers).
A wise man should not take pleasure in conflicts or enmity, and should remain stable in prosperity and adversity (विपत्तिषु/in calamities).
Content with knowledge but curious to learn more in that very field.
Forgiving, upright, skillful, and executing actions after thorough consideration (सुसमीक्षित/well-analyzed).
Modest, intelligent, highly enthusiastic (महोत्साहः/greatly energetic), sharing resources, and loving towards guests.
Having a noble lifestyle, deep-natured, virtuous, and affectionate towards dependents (आश्रित-वत्सलः/kind to refugees).
An offerer of funeral cakes to ancestors, a performer of sacrifices, and possessing a compassionate nature.
Approver of good news and deeply compassionate towards the poor (दीनानाम्/destitute individuals).
A provider of solace to the frightened, and a pacifier of the angry (क्रुद्धानाम्/enraged ones).
One who speaks first with a smiling face, possesses good character, and worships worshipful entities.
Elders and respected people should be honored in increasing order of their wealth, relationships, age, knowledge, and conduct.
An enemy of oneself, a lawbreaker, a fool, and one who has abandoned the right path.
Such a person should be completely ignored, as they are merely fuel for the flames of hell (नरक/perdition).
One should speak words that are righteous, meaningful, pleasant, true, measured, and wholesome (पथ्यं/salutary).
One should not underestimate oneself, nor praise oneself excessively, nor torture oneself.
One should not disrespect those who are inferior in livelihood, physical parts, strength, or knowledge.
One should not hurt others' feelings, nor be cruel, sharp, or a cause of affliction (उपतापवान्/distress creator).
One should feel jealous of the cause of success (virtue) but not of the fruits, and should not commit sin even towards a sinner.
One should not raise a staff against another, nor strike them in anger.
Except for a son or a disciple who deserves discipline, and that too only for their own benefit (हिताय/for welfare).
One should not indulge in wild or vulgar actions involving dancing, instrumental music, or singing.
Known for well-combed hair, controlled speech, proper attire, peacefulness, and comforting words.
One should not touch the upper parts of the body above the navel with the lower garments (अधर-वाससा/undergarments).
One should not delay the purification process after engaging in sexual intercourse (मिथुनी-भूय/having copulated).
One should not laugh, sneeze, belch, or yawn without covering the mouth (असंवृत-मुखो/uncovered mouth).
One should not scratch their own head using both hands simultaneously.
One should not carry a heavy load on the head along with fire and water at the same time.
One should not pick the nose, nor grind or gnash the teeth.
Avoid scratching, cutting, piercing, cracking, or rubbing the body parts unnecessarily.
Do not make sounds using the mouth, body limbs, or nails, whether there is work or no work.
Do not place one foot over the other, do not scratch them, and do not clean them improperly.
Do not place both feet on a bronze vessel, nor stretch them out while sitting.
Regularly keep the nails, feet, and waste passages clean (निर्मलान्/spotless).
One should not worship or sit near fire that is not well-kindled, nor do so when unclean.
Not facing the wind, not uncovered, not exhausted, and not with an distracted mind (अन्य-मानसः/mind elsewhere).
One should not blow the fire with the mouth, nor jump over it, nor treat it lowly, nor touch it with feet.
Do not look continuously at objects that are moving rapidly, microscopic, or unpleasant.
Nor look at unholy things, feces, urine, or an uncleaned mirror (दर्पणम्/looking glass).
Do not look at the sun when it is rising, setting, scorching hot at midday, or reflected in water.
Or when it is eclipsed, brilliant, or partially hidden by a cloth.
Nor look at any other highly luminous object, nor look directly at the face of an angry teacher (गुरोः मुखं/preceptor's face).
Do not look at a woman who is discharging fluids, menstruating (उदक्यां/in periods), naked, or engaged with another man.
Do not sleep, eat, or drink while facing the wind, or while sneezing, yawning, or in an uncomfortable posture.
One should not sleep on a single bed, nor eat food together with her.
One should protect her without being overly jealous, but should avoid a woman of loose character (स्वैरिणीं/wanton woman).
One should not look at the stars, Rahu, the moon, or the sun while remaining unwashed after eating.
Nor travel, nor study, nor sleep, nor touch the head in that unclean state.
Do not point out to another person a cow that is feeding her calf or grazing (चरंतीं/pasturing).
Do not point out solar or lunar halos, meteors, or rainbows (शतक्रतु-धनूंषि/Indra's bows).
Do not perform any other work while worshipping deities, or when cows and rain are pouring down.
Do not count or announce the lunar dates of the fortnight, nor point out the stars casually.
Do not disclose one's own birth sign, birth star, secret wealth, or family defects (मलं/blemishes) at home.
Do not publicize one's insult, nor the loss of affection from the master (प्रभोः/ruler or boss).
Avoid the head-on wind, strong sun, dust, frost, and harsh winds (परुष-अनिलान्/rough breezes).
Avoid sneezing, belching, coughing, sleeping, eating, or sexual intercourse in a crooked posture.
Avoid making sounds while passing wind, and avoid speaking with wild movements of hands, eyebrows, or eyes.
Avoid sitting under the shadow of a riverbank, drinking alcohol, or going near wild beasts with fangs or horns.
Avoid serving low or uncultured people, or over-shrewd persons, and avoid conflict with superiors (उत्तमैः/excellent individuals).
Avoid eating food, sexual intercourse, sleeping, studying, or deep contemplation during the morning and evening twilights.
Avoid boasting about one's health, long life, wealth, knowledge, good status, or respect.
Avoid passing between water, fire, and worshipful entities, and avoid walking through smoke or near a dead body (शव-आश्रयं/cadaver site).
Avoid excessive addiction to liquor, blind trust, and giving absolute independence to women.
One should not reside even for a single day in a house or architecture condemned by the architectural scriptures.
Nor in a region highly prevalent with diseases, nor in a place devoid of a physician (अवैद्यं/doctorless), nor in a state without a ruler.
Nor in a place crowded with half-educated people, or afflicted by calamities, or on a barren mountain.
One should reside where there is plenty of water, medicines, sacrificial wood, flowers, grass, and fuel (इंधने/firewood).
Where food is easily available, safe, beautiful, and in a city adorned by scholars (पंडितैः/wise men).
One should not disrespect human laws, realized souls, or the authentic scriptures (शास्त्राणां/scientific texts).
One should be a worshipper of the three goals of life (virtue, wealth, pleasure) and respect people according to their worth.
Observing the ten paths of righteousness, one should conquer the internal enemies (नाभ्यन्तरान् अरीन्/inner foes like lust, anger, greed).
Violence, theft, unlawful sexual desire, backbiting, harsh speech, and falsehood (अनृतं/untruth).
Incoherent chattering, malice, covetousness, and distorted perception.
These ten types of sinful actions (पापं कर्म/evil deeds) relating to body, speech, and mind should be abandoned.
One should avoid earning wealth (श्रियः/fortune) through actions that cause harm or injury to others.
Even if wealth is not achieved, one should not accept riches that bring multiple miseries (बव्हनर्थानाम्/many disasters).
By doing so, one effortlessly attains heaven, liberation, and supreme glories (विभवान्/opulences).
After eating a light and wholesome meal in the evening, keeping the mind composed and pure.
Remembering the creator or teacher, one should then retire to one's bed (स्व-शय्यां/own cot).
In a clean place that is not crowded, accompanied by two or three attendants (परिचारकः/helpers).
Using a proper pillow, well-covered with sheets, wide, even, and comfortable.
One should use a bed or seat that is knee-high, soft, and auspicious (शुभं/benign).
Sleeping with the head facing east or south, and not pointing the feet towards the teachers or elders.
During the early and late parts of the night, one should contemplate only upon righteousness (धर्मम्/virtuous principles).
In this way, one should always extract the essence of the body from this transient life.
Fearing death at every single moment, which comes due to improper or careless actions.
For a person possessing health (आरोग्य/disease-free state), wealth, wisdom, youth, and righteous actions (धर्म-क्रियावतः/virtuous deeds).
A happy (सुखम्/comfortable) and wholesome life is described, whereas the opposite is true in contrary conditions (विपर्य्यये/in reverse situations).
The king is called the repository of all brilliance on earth (भुवि/on the ground).
Therefore, without harboring any defect in the mind, one should serve him with devotion (भक्तिमान्/devotedly).
Sitting cross-legged on the seat, leaning inappropriately, mocking, arguments, spitting, and yawning.
And all actions that exceed one's normal nature should be avoided in his presence.
Indeed, thoroughly examining all the diverse and various situations, one should act for one's own welfare.
Whatever other path is available in this world containing wholesome advice, one should follow that too.
Thus, one who follows this conduct becomes a source of livelihood for all living beings, possesses a celebrated mass of vast virtues, and is protected by the gods.
Living for more than a hundred years, liberated, performing pious deeds, such a person attains a good destination and finds satisfaction even upon the destruction of the body.
Thus ends the third chapter named Daily Routine (दिनचर्या/daily lifestyle regimen).




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter on seasonal routine (ऋतुचर्या अध्याय/regimen according to seasons).
Time (काल/duration) indeed is Lord, without beginning or end (अनादि निधन/eternal), which follows accumulated actions (कर्म अनुसारी/past deeds), under whose influence the sun and others, and sky and others which are specific great elements (महाभूत/five elements), transform accordingly, becoming the clear cause (कारणत्वम्/causative factor) for birth, death, seasons, tastes (रस/flavors), potency (वीर्य/strength), doshas (दोष/bodily humors), and the decay or prosperity of body strength of living beings.
That time is divided twelve-fold (द्वादशधा विभज्यते/twelve types) into Matra, Kastha, Kala, Nadika, Muhurta, Yama, day-and-night (अहोरात्र/24 hours), fortnight (पक्ष/fifteen days), month (मास/thirty days), solstice (अयन/six months), and year (वर्ष/twelve months).
There, the time taken for a single blink of an eye (अक्षि निमेषः/eyeblink) is called one Matra.
Fifteen such Matras make one Kastha.
Thirty such Kasthas make one Kala.
Those Kalas along with their tenth part taken twenty times make twenty Nadikas.
Two Nadikas together make one Muhurta.
In equal night and day positions, four minus one-quarter (पाद ऊनाः/three and three-quarters) parts make one Yama.
By four such Yamas, a complete day and night (अहोरात्र/24 hours) is formed.
Fifteen days and nights make one fortnight (पक्ष/two weeks).
Two fortnights make one month (मास/four weeks).
That month ends with the bright fortnight (शुक्लांतः/Shukla Paksha end).
By those months starting with Margashirsha, taken in pairs of two, six seasons (षड् ऋतवः/seasonal cycles) named Hemanta, Shishira, Vasanta, Grishma, Varsha, and Sharad are formed in sequence.
Among them, the three seasons starting with Shishira are the sun's northern solstice (उत्तरायण/northward movement) and period of absorption (आदान काल/dehydration period), and the remaining are the southern solstice (दक्षिणायन/southward movement) and period of liberation (विसर्ग काल/hydration period).
Those two periods, absorption and liberation, together make one year (वर्ष/twelve months).
Out of those two, the period of absorption (आदान काल/taking away energy) is fiery (आग्नेयम्/hot and dry).
In that period indeed, due to the nature and path of time, the sun with its extremely hot network of rays, and by its contact, the winds become intensely dry (तीव्र रूक्षाः/highly dehydrating), drying up the cool lunar qualities (सोमजम् गुणम्/cooling properties) and absorbing the moisture (स्नेहम्/unctuousness) of the world, and due to dryness generated by seasonal sequence, they increase the dry tastes like bitter (तिक्त), astringent (कषाय), and pungent (कटु), bringing weakness (दौर्बल्यम्/loss of strength) to men.
The period of liberation (विसर्ग काल/giving energy) however is gentle and cooling (सौम्यः/lunar nature).
In that period also, when the sun is in the southern solstice and its intensity is countered by the seasonal path, clouds, wind, and rain, and when the moon has unhindered strength (अव्याहत बल/maximum power) and is constantly nourishing the world with its cool rays, and the heat of the earth is pacified by the rainwater of Indra, un-dry tastes (अरूक्षाः रसाः/moist flavors) increase namely sour (अम्ल), salty (लवण), and sweet (मधुर) in sequence, and the strength (बलम्/immunity) of men is accumulated.
And there is a verse on this subject (भवति च अत्र/as stated here).
In Hemanta and Shishira seasons, due to the watery nature (आप्यम्/moisture), the strength during liberation and absorption periods is at its peak (विसर्ग आदानयोः बलम्/maximum strength).
In Sharad and Vasanta, the strength is medium (मध्यम्/moderate), and it is poor or low (हीनम्/minimum) in Varsha and Grishma.
The directions and the sun appear smoky and grey (धूम धूम्राः/dust-laden), dim, and surrounded by circles blurred by frost (तुषार/fog).
The northern direction (उत्तरः/north wind) causes goosebumps (रोमहर्षणः/shivering) due to the coldness of the wind.
The forests are brightly illuminated with the blossoming flowers of Lodhra, Priyangu, Punnaga, and Lavali.
Elephants, goats, buffaloes, horses, crows, and pigs become proud and highly energetic (दृप्ताः/vigorous).
The rivers are covered with sheets of snow (हिमानी पटल/ice layers), and fishes and birds remain hidden within them.
The rivers are filled with vapor (सबाष्पाः/misty) and look warm, and the water of wells is warm (सोष्माणः/heated) at the arrival of winter.
The body heat (देह ऊष्माणः/internal temperature) enters deep inside in winter, being struck by the cold wind outside.
In the stomach, this concentrated heat makes the digestive fire (अनलम्/jatharagni) extremely strong (प्रवलम्/powerful).
In the liberation period (विसर्ग/winter season), the digestive fire of strong individuals is generally capable of digesting heavy and large quantities of food due to its natural strength.
Therefore, one should use nourishing (बृंहणानि/heavy and tissue-building) food and drinks to satisfy that digestive fire.
Otherwise, lacking fuel (अनिंधनः/without food), it might exhaust the body due to its highly inflamed state (अति उदीर्णतया/over-activation).
It may even digest the body tissues (धातून/body elements) of this person, and then, due to their depletion, the wind element (मरुत्/vata dosha) gets aggravated.
That wind, which travels along with the heat, gets severely vitiated, being cold, especially in the cold season (शीते विशेषतः/specifically in winter).
Therefore, in winter, one should enjoy unctuous (स्निग्ध/rich and oily) foods, and sweet (स्वादु), sour (अम्ल), and salty (लवण) tastes.
One should consume the meat of animals that live in burrows (बिलेशय/burrowing), aquatic animals (औदक/water-dwelling), marshy land animals (आनूप/wetland), and animals that snatch their food (प्रसह/birds of prey).
One should consume these meats, products made of jaggery (गुड/sugar cane concentrates) and flour, and freshly prepared wines (अभिनव मद्य/new alcohol).
One should use black gram (माष/urad dal), sugarcane products, milk modifications (क्षीर विकृति/dairy products), animal fat (वसा), oil, and freshly harvested rice (नव ओदन).
One should adopt exercise (व्यायाम), dry herbal powder massage (उद्वर्तन), oil massage (अभ्यंग), sweating therapy (स्वेद), medicated smoking, collyrium (अंजन), and sunbathing (आतप).
One should use lukewarm water (सुख उदकम्/comfortably warm water) for cleansing routines, and stay in underground rooms (भूमि गर्भ गृह/basements).
One should use a bed equipped with an earthen fire-pot (सांगारयान/heating brazier) and well-prepared with thick carpets and blankets.
The body should be anointed with saffron (कुंकुम), and light or heavy agaru (अगुरू/aloeswood) paste.
Covered with light but warm blankets, one should sleep at the proper time in rooms fragrant with incense (धूप/fumigation).
One should keep away the winter wind and cold through physical contact with well-nourished women (पीन अंगना/plump partners).
In Shishira season, the cold is even greater (शीतम् अधिकम्/intense cold), being born from clouds, wind, and rain.
And the dryness is also higher due to the onset of the absorption period, therefore the earlier mentioned regimen (पूर्व विधि/hemanta routine) must be followed even more strictly.
In Vasanta season, the southern breeze (दक्षिणः वायुः/malaya marut) blows, and the sun has coppery-red rays.
The trees have fresh sprouts, bark, and leaves, and all directions look completely clean (अमलाः/clear).
The forest ranges are beautifully adorned with rows of Kimshuka, Ashoka, mango (चूत), and other trees.
The environment is filled with the sweet melodies and noisy sweet chatter (कल कोलाहल/buzzing and chirping) of cuckoo birds and swarms of bees.
The kapha dosha accumulated (संचितः श्लेष्मा/stored phlegm) during Shishira season is liquefied and excited by the hot rays of the sun.
Then, suppressing the digestive fire (प्रबाधमानः अग्निम्/weakening metabolism), it produces many diseases.
Therefore, in this season, sharp therapies like therapeutic emesis (वमन/vomiting), medicated smoking, gargling (गंडूष), and nasal drops (नावन/nasya) should be done.
One should utilize exercise (व्यायाम), herbal paste massage (उद्वर्तन), honey (क्षौद्र), barley (यव), wheat (गोधूम), and meat of arid land animals (जांगल मांस).
One should spend time with good friends, in gardens (उद्यान/parks), and with beautiful, charming young women.
After taking a bath, well-decorated, wearing garlands (स्त्रग्वी/flower wreaths), and anointed with sandalwood and agaru pastes.
One should drink from various beautiful vessels adorned with mango leaves and blue lotuses.
One should drink pure and harmless fermented decoctions like Asava, Arishta, Sidhu, Mardvika, and Madhava (विभिन्न मद्य/types of ayurvedic wines).
One should drink water boiled with Musta and ginger (शुंठी अंबु/processed water), or extract-water, or honey-mixed water.
One should completely avoid heavy (गुरु), cold (शीत), daytime sleeping (दिवा स्वप्न), and unctuous, sour, and sweet foods.
In Grishma season, the sun has piercingly hot rays like an flax flower, and the world is inflamed as if by a forest fire (दाव दीपितः/blazing bushfire).
The directions seem to burn, the earth scorches, and the wind is not comfortable from any side.
Due to the hot wind, scorching sun, and excessive sweating, living beings look as if they are suffering from fever (ज्वरिताः इव/feverish).
The water bodies are made muddy and dirty (कलुषी कृताः/turbid) by tall elephants and buffaloes distressed by the extreme heat.
The water of rivers has been severely depleted by the heaps of ember-like rays of the sun (दिवाकर कर अंगार/burning sunbeams).
The rivers have highly exposed high banks (प्रवृद्ध रोधसः/shrunken water lines), and the trees are devoid of shade.
The trees have shed their old leaves and are marked with dry bark and withered creepers.
Because at that time, the sun very strongly absorbs the moisture (स्नेहम्/unctuousness) of the universe.
Therefore, one should completely abandon exercise (व्यायाम), exposure to sun, and pungent (कटु), sour (अम्ल), salty (लवण), and hot foods.
Wine (मद्य) should not be consumed, or if taken, only in very small quantities, or mixed with a large amount of water (सुबहु वारि/highly diluted).
Otherwise, that wine causes emaciation (शोष), looseness of tissues (शैथिल्य), burning sensation (दाह), and fainting or confusion (मोह).
One should consume things stored in new earthen pots (नव मृद् भाजन/clay vessels) and which are fragrant and pleasing.
One should drink refreshing syrups (पानक) along with churned flours (मंथ/sattu), which are rich in sugar (सिता आढ्यानि/highly sweetened) and ice-cold.
One should eat sweet (स्वादु), cold, and liquid (द्रव) food, and the meat of animals and birds from arid lands (जांगल).
One should consume Shali rice, milk (क्षीर), ghee (घृत), raisins (द्राक्षा), coconut water, and sugar (शर्करा).
One should enjoy the breeze from fans made of palm leaves (ताल वृंत अनिल/hand fans), pearl necklaces, and garlands made of lotuses and lilies.
One should enjoy the company of beautiful women wearing delicate bracelets of lotus stalks (मृणाल वलय) and anointed with sandalwood paste.
One should resort to lakes (सरांसि), stepwells (वापी), rivers, and cool forest groves.
One should wear fragrant and very light clothes (सु लघूनि वासांसि/fine cotton garments).
During the day, one should sleep in a shower-house (धारा गृह/water-spray room) where water drops are falling from machines.
And at night, one should sleep on the open terrace (आकाश तलके/under the night sky) spread with fragrant flowers.
Anointed with camphor and sandalwood on wet body parts, and keeping sexual indulgence very minimal (विरल अनंग संगमः/rare intercourse).
In Varsha season, the western wind (वारुणः वायुः/rain-bearing breeze) blows, and all types of vegetation begin to sprout.
The sky is gradually made cloudy and hazy by dark clouds looking like broken blue sapphires.
The continuous flow of fresh water in long stepwells looks like a row of ladders leading to the sky.
The lotuses in the ponds are damaged and ruined by the forceful impact of heavy rain showers.
The rivers take the massive shape of an ocean, and the earth becomes indistinguishable between land and water (अव्यक्त जल स्थला/flooded terrain).
The earth is filled with the deep rumbling of clouds (मंद्र स्तनित/low thunder) and the calls of peacocks and frogs.
The environment is illuminated by rain-bugs (इंद्रगोप/cochineal insects), fragments of rainbows, and flashes of lightning.
The ground is covered all around with dark green grass and shines with mushrooms (शिलींध्र) and Kutaj flowers.
At that time, when the body strength is low due to the absorption period, and the digestive fire is weak and further afflicted.
By the vitiated Vata and other doshas caused by rain, earth-vapors (भू बाष्प), and the sour digestion of water (तोय अम्ल पाक).
One should undergo the proper purification therapies (संशोधन क्रमः/detoxification) and then regularly employ enema therapy (बस्ती कर्म).
One should eat old Shali rice, wheat, and barley along with prepared lentil soups (यूष) or meat soups (रस).
One should consume harmless wine, Arishta, or Mardvika, and drink only a small quantity of water.
One should drink pure rainwater (दिव्यम्), boiled well-water, or water from clean lakes and ponds.
On days disturbed by heavy rain and wind, the food eaten must conquer moisture (क्लेद) and Vata dosha.
One should enjoy food that is well-dried, light (लघु), unctuous, hot, sour, and salty.
Generally, when dense clouds cover the sky, the food and drinks should be mixed with honey (सक्षौद्रम्) and be thick in consistency.
One should stay in places free from crawling reptiles (असरीसृप), earth-vapors, cold wind, and water spray.
One should stay in a house equipped with a heating brazier, and which is entirely free from gnats, mosquitoes, and rats.
One should fondle scrubbing (प्रघर्ष), herbal massage (उद्वर्तन), bathing, medicated smoke, and the fragrance of agaru wood.
One should travel on excellent female elephants or chief vehicles, beautifully adorned in colorful clothes.
One should completely avoid river water, churned flour drinks (उद मन्थ), day sleeping, excessive liquid intake, and sexual intercourse.
One should avoid getting feet wet in frost, physical exercise, and exposure to the morning sun rays.
In Sharad season, the sky has clean white clouds, and the earth is only slightly marked with mud.
The land is beautifully adorned with sunlight, Kasha grass flowers, Saptaparna trees, white water-lilies, and Shali rice fields.
With the departure of the dense masses of clouds, the sun scatters very sharp rays (तीक्ष्ण किरणः/strong radiation).
The directions turn tawny-brown, become extremely clear, and are crowded with flying lines of Cranes (क्रौंच माला/migrating birds).
Due to the movement and friction of groups of swans and fishes hidden inside the lotuses.
The clean lakes have high waves created by the breaking of ripples (तरंग भंग/wavy waters).
In humans whose bodies are accustomed to the cold of the rainy season, suddenly by the rays of the sun.
Being scorched by that heat, the Pitta dosha which was accumulated previously (संचितम् पूर्वम्/stored in monsoon) now gets highly aggravated.
Intake of ghee processed with bitter herbs (तिक्तैः हविः पानम्/medicated ghee), purgation therapy (विरेकः), and bloodletting (अस्त्र श्रुतिः) are always recommended.
One should take cold, light food and drinks, featuring astringent (कषाय), sweet (स्वादु), and bitter (तिक्तक) tastes.
One should consume Shali rice, Shashtika rice, wheat, barley, green gram (मुद्ग), sugar candy (सिता), and honey.
One should eat pointed gourd (पटोल), amla (आमलकम्), raisins, and when experiencing strong hunger, meat of arid land animals (जांगल).
During the day, heated by the rays of the sun, and during the night, cooled by the rays of the moon.
Detoxified by the rising of the star Agastya, such water is purified.
This water, clear and naturally processed over time, is like nectar (अमृत उपमम्/divine drink) for drinking purpose.
Where the lines of hovering bees are wandering due to the flapping of wings of groups of swans.
One should swim or bathe in such lakes beautifully occupied by excellent lotuses.
One should wear light, clean clothes, wear flower garlands, and apply the cooling paste of Ushir (खस/vetiver).
One should enjoy the cool rays of the moon in the evening, sitting on the terrace of a palace (सौधम् आश्रितः/mansion rooftop).
One should achieve satisfaction through food, but must avoid alkalis (अक्षार), animal fat, oil, and the eastern wind (पुरः अनिल).
One should completely avoid strong alcohol (तीक्ष्ण मद्य), day sleeping, and exposure to frost or morning mist.
Daily, one should practice the usage of all six tastes, but with their respective dominance in each specific season.
The last seven days of the passing season and the first seven days of the coming season are known as the seasonal transition period (ऋतु संधि/inter-seasonal junction).
There, the routine of the previous season should be abandoned, and the routine of the next season should be adopted gradually.
Because diseases born from unsuitability (असात्म्य जाः/health issues due to sudden change) may occur by sudden abandonment or sudden adoption of habits.
In these regular seasons, this kind of regimen is directed for maintaining the health of living beings.
In seasons showing unnatural or altered characteristics, a regimen based on contrary understanding (विरुद्ध ज्ञानिकः विधिः/adaptive routine) is advised.
Based on the characteristics of the months and zodiac signs, whatever three features are described for a season.
According to the intensity of that season, one should follow the respective routine there, based on its strength.
Thus ends the fourth chapter named Ritu-charya (ऋतुचर्या नाम चतुर्थः अध्यायः/the chapter on seasonal routine).




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on the prevention of diseases (रोग अनुत्पादनीयम्/non-production of illnesses).
One should not suppress (धारयेत्/forcefully hold back) the natural urges (वेगान्/body impulses) of flatus (वात/gas), feces (विट्/stool), urine (मूत्र/piss), sneezing (क्षव/छींक), thirst (तृट्/प्यास), and hunger (क्षुधाम्/भूख).
As well as sleep (निद्रा/नींद), cough (कास/खांसी), exertion breathlessness (श्रम श्वासात्/हाफंना), yawning (जृम्भा/उबासी), tears (अश्रु/आंसू), vomiting (छर्दि/उल्टी), and semen (रेतसाम्/वीर्य).
By the suppression (रोधेन/रुकावट) of flatus (अधोवातस्य/पाद), phantom tumor (गुल्म/पेट की गांठ), upward movement of gas (उदावर्त/bloating), pain (रुक्/दर्द), and exhaustion (क्लमाः/थकान) occur.
And also obstruction (सङ्ग/रुकावट) of flatus, urine, and feces, impaired vision (दृष्टि वध/कमजोरी), loss of digestive fire (अग्नि वध/भूख न लगना), and heart disease (हृद् गदाः/दिल की बीमारी).
The oleation (स्नेह/तेल मालिश) and sudation therapy (स्वेद विधिः/पसीना निकालना) are beneficial there, along with suppositories (वर्त्तयः/बत्ती) and suitable food (भोजनानि/खाना).
And drinks (पानानि/पेय पदार्थ), enema therapy (बस्तयः/एनिमा), and treatment that facilitates downward movement of gas (वात अनुलोमनम्/गैस पास करना) are recommended (शस्तम्/उचित).
By suppression of feces (शकृतः/मल), calf muscle cramps (पिण्डिका उद्वेष्ट/पिंडली का ऐंठन), common cold (प्रतिश्याय/जुकाम), and headache (शिरो रुजः/सिर दर्द) occur.
Along with upward movement of air (ऊर्ध्वं वायुः/डकार), cutting pain in the rectum (परिकर्तो/गुदा में कटना), and oppression of the heart (हृदयस्य उपरोधनम्/छाती में जकड़न).
And passing of feces through the mouth (मुखेन विट् प्रवृत्तिः/उल्टी में मल आना), and all previously mentioned diseases (पूर्व उक्ताः आमयाः/पहले कहे गए रोग) are remembered (स्मृताः/जाने जाते हैं).
Body aches (अङ्ग भङ्ग/बदन दर्द), urinary stones (अश्मरी/पथरी), and pain (वेदनाः/दर्द) in the bladder (बस्ति/मूत्राशय), penis (मेढ्र/लिंग), and groin (वंक्षण/जांघ का जोड़) occur.
These happen from suppression of urine (मूत्र रोधात्/पेशाब रोकना), along with most of the previously mentioned diseases; now hear their remedy (औषधम्/इलाज).
Suppositories (वर्ति/बत्ती), oil massage (अभ्यङ्ग/मालिश), tub bath (अवगाहाः/टब बाथ), sweating therapy (स्वेदनम्/सेक), and enema treatment (बस्ति कर्म/एनिमा) should be done.
And food and drinks (अन्न पानम्/खान-पान) that break the feces (विट् भेदि/मल ढीला करने वाला) should be given for wasting diseases (यक्ष्मसु/टीबी या कमजोरी) arising from constipation (विट् विबन्धात्/कब्ज).
For conditions arising from urine suppression (मूत्र जेषु/पेशाब रोकने से पैदा हुए), drinking medicated ghee (घृतम्/घी) before food (प्राक् भक्तात्/खाली पेट) is praised (शस्यते/उत्तम माना गया है).
And also at the end of digestion (जीर्ण अन्तिकम्/खाना पचने के बाद), administration in a large dose (उत्तमया मात्रया/भारी खुराक) in two ways is recommended.
This is known as the sandwich dose therapy (अवपीडकम्/दबाकर देना); now further hear the effects of suppression (धारणात्/रोकने से).
Of eructation (उद्गारस्य/डकार), loss of appetite (अरुचिः/अरुचि), tremors (कम्पः/कांपना), and obstruction (विबन्धः/रुकावट) in the heart and chest (हृदय उरसोः/सीना).
Flatulence (आध्मान/पेट फूलना), cough (कास/खांसी), and hiccups (हिध्माः/हिचकी) occur, and the treatment (भेषजम्/दवाई) there is similar to hiccup treatment (हिध्मावत्/हिचकी जैसा).
