In brief, all these diseases are broadly classified into two categories: present deed-derived (प्रत्युत्पन्न कर्मज/present deeds) and past life deed-derived (पूर्व कर्मज/past karma).
Present deed-derived diseases arise from improper actions performed in this present life, such as disregarding medical advice, breaking health rules, or indulging in forbidden practices.
Past life deed-derived diseases arise from sinful deeds performed in previous births (जन्मान्तर/past life).
This past karmic origin is called providential fate (दैव/fate), previously classified as fixed (नियत/fixed) or variable (अनियत/variable).
Therefore, diseases with visible immediate causes are present deed-derived (प्रत्युत्पन्न कर्मज/present actions).
Diseases lacking visible causes arise from past karmic fate (दैवजन्मन/karmic origin).
Diseases produced by minor causes but producing severe pathology arise from both present deeds and past karmic fate (उभयात्मक/dual origin).
Diseases born of present deeds are cured by adopting appropriate opposing diets, medicines, and regimens (प्रतिपक्ष/counteractive therapy).
Diseases born of past karmic fate are cured only when the underlying karmic energy is exhausted through pious acts, atonement, and spiritual pacification.
Diseases born of combined origins subside through the destruction of both vitiated humors and past karmic remnants.
Other scholars state that present actions performed by others can also cause disease.
This secondary external influence is called affliction caused by others (पराभिसंस्कार/external influence).
Regarding this, scholars state as follows:
If all results arose solely from one's own past deeds, why would even a wise man react with joy or anger toward the beneficial or harmful acts done to him by another person?
Thus, these diseases, though classified into two or three primary categories, manifest in diverse forms.
Furthermore, according to the involvement of individual humors, diseases are classified into seven humor-derived types (सप्तविध/sevenfold types).
This entire spectrum of treatable diseases relies upon following the authoritative medical guidelines of Ayurveda (आयुर्वेद उपदेश/ayurvedic guidance).
Because even a severe disease with fixed causes quickly yields to pain relief when the patient strictly follows the physician's instructions while vital life capacity persists.
If left untreated, almost every disease destroys life prematurely (अकाण्डे/untimely death).
Even if a disease begins to subside on its own due to mild etiology or favorable fate, applying the 16-fold ideal clinical treatment remedies (षोडशगुण/16 clinical qualities) cures the patient much faster and without distress.
When past karmic fate is non-binding (अनियत फल/variable fate), disease finds no opportunity to invade a person who consistently practices wholesome living (हित सेवन/wholesome habits).
Therefore, a self-controlled wise person should never treat wholesome and unwholesome practices as equal under any circumstance.
Furthermore, diseases are classified into three degrees of severity: mild (मृदु/mild), moderate (मध्य/moderate), and severe (अधिमात्र/intense).
Diseases showing few mild symptoms are mild (मृदु/mild); those showing moderate symptoms are moderate (मध्य/moderate); those presenting complete severe symptoms are severe (अधिमात्र/intense).
These are further categorized into four prognostic types (easily curable, difficult to cure, manageable, and incurable) as explained previously.
Though diseases have endless subdivisions, they never deviate from the two primary categories: endogenous (निज/endogenous) and exogenous (आगन्तु/exogenous).
Endogenous diseases (निज/endogenous) originate internally from vitiated bodily humors (दोष/bodily humors).
In endogenous diseases, imbalance of Vata and other humors occurs first, followed by pain and physical distress.
Exogenous diseases (आगन्तु/exogenous) originate from external causes such as trauma, poisons, or severe injuries.
In exogenous diseases, pain and tissue damage occur first, followed subsequently by humoral imbalance.
Pain and disease symptoms persist and grow exclusively through the secondary vitiation of bodily humors.
Thus, no disease process can continue or spread without the involvement of vitiated humors.
The only distinction between endogenous and exogenous diseases lies in the sequence of occurrence (पौर्वापर्य/chronological order).
Therefore, diseases are unified by the common presence of pain, yet infinite in variety due to specific etiology, seat, symptoms, pathology, name, pain nature, potency, and treatment.
Due to their infinite varieties, diseases should be classified and diagnosed strictly according to the specific signs of vitiated humors (दोष लिंग/humoral signs).
Bodily humors (दोष/bodily humors) are indeed the sole primary cause of all internal diseases.
Just as a bird flying across the sky all day cannot fly beyond its own shadow,
Or just as all diverse objects in the universe cannot exist outside the three cosmic qualities (गुणत्रय/sattva rajas tamas),
So also all diverse diseases in the body cannot exist outside the three bodily humors (दोषत्रय/three humors).
And just as lightning and rain occur in the sky, driven by specific meteorological causes,
Or as waves and bubbles arise necessarily from water,
So also diseases necessarily arise from the vitiation of bodily humors.
The primary causes of humoral vitiation are threefold: unwholesome sensory contact (असात्म्येन्द्रियार्थ संयोग/unwholesome sensory contact), intellectual blasphemy (प्रज्ञापराध/intellectual error), and seasonal transformation (परिणाम/seasonal time).
Each of these three causes is further divided threefold: excessive use (अतियोग/excessive use), non-use (अयोग/non-use), and perverse use (मिथ्यायोग/perverse use).
Excessive contact (अतियोग/excessive contact) is staring at intensely bright objects, listening to deafening sounds, or overusing any sense organ.
Non-use (अयोग/non-use) is total absence or insufficient contact between sense organs and their objects.
Perverse use (मिथ्यायोग/perverse contact) includes looking at frightening distorted shapes, listening to screeching sounds, smelling foul odors, consuming unwholesome foods, or exposing body to unnatural cold, heat, or touch.
Excessive action (अतियोग/excessive action) is over-exertion in speech, thought, or physical body.
Non-action (अयोग/non-action) is total suppression or absence of physical, vocal, and mental activities.
Perverse action (मिथ्यायोग/perverse action) includes suppressing natural urges, forcible urge ejection, improper body posture, violent physical assault, harsh speech, untruthfulness, and mental hostility.
In seasonal time (काल/season), excessive cold, heat, or rain is excessive time (अतियोग/excessive season); lack of expected seasonal weather is non-use (अयोग/deficient season).
Perverse seasonal manifestation (मिथ्यायोग/perverse season) is manifestation of unseasonable opposite weather patterns.
These three fundamental factors—unwholesome sensory contact, intellectual blasphemy, and seasonal transformation—act singly or in combination to excite bodily humors.
When aggravated by these factors, the bodily humors produce various diseases (रोग/diseases).
Without such causative factors, bodily humors do not undergo pathological aggravation.
Hence, bodily humors are invaginated as indispensable factors in every physical disease (शारीर विकार/bodily illness).
Nor do diseases arise directly from sensory contacts without first vitiating the humors.
Therefore, medical authorities declare vitiated bodily humors to be the sole direct cause of internal diseases.
Unwholesome sensory contact, intellectual error, and time are classified as indirect distant causes (हेतु/cause).
Thus, a disease manifests through the combined operation of distant causes and immediate humoral vitiation.
Indeed, apart from humors, no disease process can be conceived without these primary causative errors.
Furthermore, the manifestation or non-manifestation of all diseases depends on the specific strength or weakness of etiology (निदान/cause), humor (दोष/humor), and tissue (दूष्य/tissue).
When etiology, humor, and tissue do not combine strongly, or are weak and incompatible, diseases either do not manifest, appear after long delay, remain mild, or present incomplete symptoms.
Conversely, when etiology, humor, and tissue combine strongly, diseases manifest rapidly with severe, complete symptoms.
The disease pathways (रोग मार्ग/disease pathways) are threefold: external (बाह्य/external), middle (मध्य/middle), and internal (आभ्यन्तर/internal).
The external disease pathway (बाह्य मार्ग/external pathway) consists of skin (त्वच्/skin) and blood/muscle tissue elements, also designated as the peripheral limbs (शाखा/limbs).
Diseases resting on the external pathway include goiter (गंड/goiter), boils (पिटिका/pustules), warts (चर्मकील/warts), moles (व्यंग/moles), external swellings (शोथ/swelling), external piles (अर्शः/piles), erysipelas (विसर्प/erysipelas), and abscesses (विद्रधि/abscess).
The middle disease pathway (मध्य मार्ग/middle pathway) consists of vital organs (मर्म/vital organs: head, heart, bladder) and bone-joint structures.
Along with their binding ligaments (स्नायु/ligaments), blood vessels (शिरा/vessels), and tendons (कंडरा/tendons).
Diseases resting on the middle pathway include hemiplegia (पक्षवध/paralysis), tetanus (अपतानक/tetanus), facial paralysis (अर्दित/facial paralysis), consumption (राजयक्ष्मा/consumption), bone-joint pain (अस्थिसंधिशूल/joint pain), prolapse of rectum (गुदभ्रंश/rectal prolapse), and head diseases.
The internal disease pathway (आभ्यन्तर मार्ग/internal pathway) consists of the gastrointestinal tract (महास्रोतः/GI tract), body core (कोष्ठ/gut core), stomach (आमाशय/stomach), and intestines (पक्वाशय/colon).
Diseases resting on the internal pathway include fever (ज्वर/fever), diarrhea (अतिसार/diarrhea), vomiting (छर्दि/vomiting), indigestion (अलसक/indigestion), asthma (श्वास/asthma), cough (कास/cough), hiccups (हिक्का/hiccups), constipation (आनाह/flatulence constipation), abdominal dropsy (उदर/dropsy), spleen enlargement (प्लीहा/spleen enlargement), internal swellings, piles, and abdominal tumors (गुल्म/phantom tumor).
Furthermore, diseases are classified as independent primary disorders (अनुबंध्य/primary disease) or secondary associated disorders (अनुबंध/secondary disease).
Independent primary diseases (स्वतंत्र/independent) manifest clear characteristic symptoms, distinct etiology, and specific relieved states (उपशय/relieving factors).
Secondary associated diseases (परतंत्र/dependent) present reverse features, depending on the primary disease.
Similarly, bodily humors (दोष/bodily humors) are also classified as primary independent (स्वतंत्र/independent) or secondary dependent (परतंत्र/dependent).
Secondary associated conditions are of two types: preceding manifestations (पुरोगामिन्/preceding signs) and succeeding manifestations (अनुगामिन्/subsequent signs).
Preceding manifestations are termed premonitory symptoms (पूर्वरूप/premonitory signs); succeeding manifestations are termed secondary complications (उपद्रव/complications).
One should recognize these premonitory signs and complications clearly as described in clinical sections.
When the primary independent disease is cured, its secondary complications usually subside automatically.
If complications do not subside after primary cure, or if a complication becomes dangerously severe, treat the complication urgently while managing the primary disease.
Because a severe complication causes far greater pain and distress to an already weakened body.
Just as carbuncles (प्रमेहपीडका/diabetic carbuncles) severely afflict a patient already debilitated by diabetes.
Furthermore, a primary disease itself often becomes the direct cause (हेतु/cause) of another secondary disease.
As fever (ज्वर/fever) causes bleeding disorder (रक्तपित्त/bleeding disease), or bleeding disorder causes fever.
And both fever and bleeding disorder give rise to dyspnea/asthma (श्वास/asthma).
Spleen enlargement (प्लीहा/spleen enlargement) gives rise to abdominal enlargement (उदर/dropsy).
And abdominal dropsy gives rise to systemic swelling (शोथ/swelling).
Hemorrhoids (अर्शः/piles) give rise to abdominal tumors (गुल्म/tumors), dropsy (उदर/dropsy), diarrhea (अतिसार/diarrhea), and IBS (ग्रहणी/IBS).
Chronic rhinitis (प्रतिश्याय/rhinitis) gives rise to cough (कास/cough) and fever (ज्वर/fever).
Cough and fever give rise to pulmonary consumption (क्षय/consumption).
And consumption gives rise to total tissue wasting (शोष/wasting).
A single dietary/lifestyle error can cause a single disease.
Or a single error can cause multiple diseases; similarly, multiple errors can cause a single disease or multiple diseases.
Similarly, a single symptom may represent one or many diseases; multiple symptoms may represent one or many diseases.
In the same manner, a single therapeutic measure may cure one or many diseases.
Or a measure curing one disease may excite another disease.
Therefore, the prudent physician should carefully examine all disease aspects using authoritative scripture, direct observation, and inference.
When examining a patient, evaluate disease origin, etiology, anatomical seat, pain type, visual appearance, sound, smell, taste, touch, premonitory signs, complications, progression stage, prognosis, and exact designation.
When all clinical aspects are favorable, initiate treatment; otherwise, refrain from treatment in incurable states.
Direct observation (प्रत्यक्ष/direct perception) involves inspecting skin color, shape, build, shadow, stool, urine, vomitus, gurgling intestinal sounds, joint cracking, body/ulcer odor, and touch qualities (cold, hot, smooth, rough, stiff).
Interrogation (प्रश्न/interrogation) involves asking about taste in mouth, ease of vomiting, bowel habit (मृदु क्रूर कोष्ठ/bowel tendency), dream visions, personal desires, birth star, disease onset, and subjective feelings of pleasure/pain.
Patient's age (वयस्/age) is determined by direct visual observation.
Inference (अनुमान/inference) involves deducing bad skin taste when lice flee the body (यूका अपसर्पण/lice fleeing).
Inferring sweet body taste/diabetes when flies swarm around body and urine (मक्षिका उपसर्पण/flies swarming).
Inferring digestive fire by food digestion power, physical strength by exercise capacity, hidden disease by exploratory relief (उपशय अनुपशय/diagnostic relief), fatal prognosis by fatal signs (रिष्ट/fatal signs), and mental stability by close observation.
Regarding this, the following classical summary verses are stated:
A physician who fails to enter the patient's inner soul with the lamp of knowledge and intellect (ज्ञान बुद्धि दीप/lamp of knowledge),
Like a yogi entering another body, can never successfully treat diseases.
Two types of diseased patients fail to present true disease severity:
First, a patient with a severe disease who appears mildly ill due to strong mind and robust physical strength (सत्त्व देह बल/mind-body strength);
Second, a patient with a mild disease who appears severely ill due to weak mind and feeble body.
An ignorant physician (बालिश/foolish physician) gets deceived by mere external appearances.
He administers weak remedies for severe diseases, or potent drastic remedies for mild diseases.
By administering contradictory treatments, he destroys the patient's vital life force (प्राण/vital breath).
Partial knowledge can never comprehend the complete science of medicine.
Therefore, the physician should desire complete truth by deep study of classical treatises (तन्त्र अनुशीलन/textbook study).
A dedicated physician who constantly analyzes all factors in every way,
Never stumbles or fails in clinical practice, even in highly complex medical situations.
An exogenous disease eventually involves endogenous humors, and a severe endogenous disease attracts exogenous complications.
Therefore, understanding primary cause (प्रकृति/primary nature) and secondary association (अनुबंध/secondary association) thoroughly, the physician should begin treatment.
Thus ends the twenty-second chapter (द्वाविंशः अध्यायः/twenty-second chapter) named 'Differential Classification of Diseases' (रोगभेदीयः/classification of diseases) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on the principles of drug administration and dosages (भेषजावचारणीय/drug administration) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
Before administering any drug (भेषज/medicine), the physician should first carefully examine the patient (आतुर/patient).
One should ascertain in which habitat (देश/habitat region) the patient was born, raised, or fell ill.
One should examine the dietary habits (आहार/dietary habits), lifestyle (विहार/lifestyle), physical strength (बल/strength), mental constitution (सत्त्व/mental stamina), wholesome adaptations (सात्म्य/wholesome habits), preferences, prevalent diseases, and beneficial vs. harmful factors in that region.
One should determine through which specific etiological factor (निदान/etiology) the humor became aggravated, for even a single humor has multiple causes of aggravation.
Therefore, one should comprehend the aggravated humor intelligently through its specific symptoms (लक्षण/symptoms) and physiological actions (कर्म/actions).
Namely as follows:
Did Vata humor aggravate due to dry, light, or cold diet, physical overexertion, suppression of natural urges, fear, or grief?
Thereafter, administering medicine directly opposing that cause leads to rapid therapeutic success.
Here, sweet, sour, and salty tastes counteract pungent, bitter, and astringent tastes, and vice versa.
Thereafter, one should evaluate whether the etiological causes were consumed in mild, moderate, or excessive degrees.
Even for a single cause, differences in mild, moderate, or severe degrees and specific fraction-by-fraction strength of humors determine the severity of disease.
In complex multi-humoral diseases and multi-taste formulations, examine taste, humor, and specific potency individually to determine overall disease-drug interaction.
However, this rule does not apply universally in all cases.
For complex compound combinations with mutually interacting ingredients, one cannot judge overall compound effect merely by estimating individual component potencies.
In such complex formulations, understand the true drug effect by observing the overall combined formulation effect (समुदाय प्रभाव/combined action).
Furthermore, ascertain in which tissue seat or organ (धाम/ anatomical seat) the disease is currently located.
When a spreading humor lodges in its own site or vital organs like the head, the resulting illness becomes extremely difficult to cure.
Therefore, specific remedies must be selected considering the specific anatomical location (स्थान/location).
Thereafter, consider the following:
Which specific drug is suitable for this disease and patient, and in what dose, for drug potency and dosage depend on disease severity and patient strength.
Abrupt administration of overly strong purificatory drugs, fiery/airy herbs, excessive doses, cautery, alkalis, or surgery may kill a weak or feeble-minded patient.
Pacifying medicines stronger than the disease, after subduing the illness, rapidly induce another secondary disorder in the debilitated body.
Doses exceeding body strength cause exhaustion, fainting, intoxication, delusion, and weakness, while doses exceeding digestive power dampen appetite.
Furthermore:
A patient who is extremely obese, emaciated, weak, has poorly formed muscles/blood/bones, low digestion, small appetite, unwholesome diet, or lacks tissue essence cannot even endure disease severity, let alone strong drug potency.
Therefore, treat such weak patients with non-distressing, gentle, comforting medicines, gradually increasing potency without causing panic.
Patients with unstable, tender, or fearful minds, being delicate, require gentle reassurance and supportive care.
This applies even more specifically to infants and young children (शिशु/infants).
Conversely, giving weak or insufficient medicine to a strong patient with severe disease proves ineffective, merely provoking humors and aggravating illness.
Even a suitable drug should be examined in this manner:
Evaluating its taste, potency, vipaka, qualities, substance, action, specific prabhava, origin region, harvesting season, storage method, processing, combination, and exact dosage for a specific person at a specific time to eliminate or pacify humors.
Similarly, evaluate whether any other similar medicine produced such effects previously.
And observe what specific result was produced when administered with specific modifications.
For subtle variations in humor, drug, tissue, region, season, strength, digestion, diet, essence, habit, mind, constitution, and age, if unexamined, destroy the patient.
Examining these subtle factors can perplex even a highly intelligent physician, let alone a dull-witted one.
Therefore, one should constantly refine one's intellect (प्रज्ञा/intellect) through continuous study of scripture and practical experience.
Furthermore:
There exist diseases whose treatments are laid down in scripture through general rules (उत्सर्ग/general rule) and exceptions (अपवाद/exception).
Here, only a wise physician decides correctly between general rule and exception based on severity of humors.
Proper timing (काल/timing) imparts fitness and efficacy to medicine.
Timing was previously described as twofold: astrological moments (क्षण मुहूर्त/time moments) and patient disease stages.
Furthermore:
Seasonal time is threefold characterized by cold, heat, and rain.
Between cold and hot seasons, and between rainy and cold seasons, lies moderate spring (वसन्त/spring) and autumn (शरद्/autumn).
Between summer and rainy season, the onset of rain is designated as early monsoon (प्रावृट्/early monsoon).
Among the three main seasons (winter, summer, monsoon), three moderate seasons (spring, early monsoon, autumn) are chosen for purificatory therapies (शोधन/cleansing).
For purificatory cleansing, Phalguna and Chaitra months constitute Spring (वसन्त/spring).
Ashadha and Shravana months constitute Early Monsoon (प्रावृट्/early monsoon).
Kartika and Margashirsha months constitute Autumn (शरद्/autumn).
Purificatory therapies like emesis (वमन/vomiting) are administered during these moderate days, avoiding harsh seasons to prevent inadequate or excessive action.
Moderate days with mild cold, heat, and rain facilitate proper excitation and smooth evacuation of humors.
Extreme seasons naturally freeze or lock humors due to excessive cold or heat.
Thus, severe cold suppresses excited Kapha humor and neutralizes medicinal potency.
Administering medicine during severe heat induces thirst, fainting, burning heat, and exhaustion.
Therefore, in cold season, create a summer-like warm environment using wind-free shelter, heating, warm water, and warm unguents.
In hot season, create a winter-like cool environment using fountain rooms, cold water, and cooling remedies.
During heavy rains, continuous cold winds and soggy ground/water corrupt body humors.
When Kapha and Vata are excited by rainy weather, pacify them with Vata-Kapha pacifying remedies.
In a person with weak digestive fire (मंदाग्नि/low digestion) or over-sweating, purificatory therapy leads to dangerous over-evacuation.
In extreme cold with freezing winds, do not administer purificatory therapy without creating a warm environment.
In scorching heat with harsh dry winds, the body becomes over-sweated, loose, and humors melt excessively.
Even a non-heating medicine, exposed to scorching sun rays, acquires sharp hot potency causing over-evacuation.
And the patient's body suffers thirst, dizziness, and exhaustion.
Therefore, administer emesis and purgation only during moderate season intervals.
Unless the disease is a severe emergency (आत्ययिक रोग/medical emergency).
In medical emergencies, create artificial opposite seasonal qualities and carefully administer adjusted drug formulations.
In patient conditions, timing is classified as proper time (काल/proper time) or improper time (अकाल/improper time).
Namely as follows:
Whether a specific stage is the proper time or improper time for a specific medicine.
Medicine given before proper time or after time has passed is ineffective; drug administration has 11 timing schedules (एकादश काल/eleven timing schedules).
Namely as follows:
Empty stomach (अभक्त/empty stomach), before meal (प्राग्भक्त/before meal), middle of meal (मध्यभक्त/middle meal), after meal (अधोभक्त/after meal), mixed with food (समभक्त/food mixed), between meals (अंतरभक्त/between meals), sandwiched (समुद्ग/sandwiched dose), repeated frequent doses (मुहुर्मुहुः/frequent doses), with every morsel (सग्रास/with bite), between morsels (ग्रासांतर/between bites), and at night (निशि/night schedule).
Empty stomach (अभक्त/empty stomach) means taking medicine alone without any food.
Undiluted by food, it has maximum potency; given early morning in strong patients for Kapha excess when stomach channels are clear.
Other schedules like before meal (प्राग्भक्त/before meal) blend with food.
Mixing with food prevents severe bodily distress or nausea.
Before meal (प्राग्भक्त/before meal) means taking medicine immediately prior to eating.
Used for Apana Vata disorders, lower body ailments, imparting strength to lower limbs, and slimming therapy.
Middle of meal (मध्यभक्त/middle meal) means taking medicine midway during eating, for Samana Vata disorders, GI tract diseases, and Pitta ailments.
After meal (अधोभक्त/after meal) means taking medicine immediately after eating.
Given after breakfast for Vyana Vata, after dinner for Udana Vata, for upper body ailments, Kapha disorders, and weight gain.
Mixed with food (समभक्त/food mixed) means medicine cooked or stirred directly into food.
Used for children, delicate individuals, drug-averse patients, anorexia, and generalized all-body diseases.
Between meals (अंतरभक्त/between meals) means taking medicine midday after morning meal digests, followed by evening meal after medicine digests.
By this, night schedule is similarly explained.
It is indicated for heart-pleasing treatments and Vyana Vata disorders.
Sandwiched (समुद्ग/sandwiched dose) means taking medicine at the beginning and end of a light meal.
Given with light food/drinks as linctus or syrup for hiccups, tremors, convulsions, and dual upper/lower humors.
Repeated doses (मुहुर्मुहुः/frequent doses) means taking medicine repeatedly with or without food.
Used for severe asthma, cough, hiccups, thirst, vomiting, and toxic poisoning disorders.
With every morsel (सग्रास/with bite) means mixing medicine into each mouthful of food.
Between morsels (ग्रासांतर/between bites) means taking medicine between individual bites of food.
Both these schedules are used specifically for Prana Vata disorders.
With morsels, powders, linctuses, or pills are given to kindle digestion and for aphrodisiac therapy.
Between morsels, emetic powders or medicated smoke (धूम/herbal smoke) are administered in heart diseases.
At night (निशि/night schedule) schedule is indicated for diseases of head and neck (जत्रूर्ध्व रोग/head neck diseases).
In the first empty stomach schedule, avoid medicine if thirsty, over-hydrated, suffering indigestion, starving, or emaciated.
In remaining schedules, avoid unpalatable, unwholesome, excessively sharp, pungent smelling, or excessive doses.
Here are the summary verses:
First examine the disease, thereafter examine the medicine.
Thereafter, the physician should perform treatment based on complete knowledge.
Even a cured disease recurs due to minor causes,
For residual humors in weakened pathways act like a hidden spark of fire.
Therefore, follow up with safe continuous therapeutic administration.
Even for proven classical formulations, maintaining consistency ensures stability.
If wholesome medicine becomes unpalatable due to continuous intake or natural taste,
Make it palatable again using diverse pharmaceutical processing methods (कल्पना/pharmaceutical form).
Pleasing the patient's mind generates contentment, energy, appetite, and physical strength.
It enables comfortable intake and leads to complete eradication of disease.
Anorexia caused by food aversion, humoral loss, or disease imbalance
Should be treated by an expert physician using wholesome prepared food formulations.
Accumulated humors from cold, heat, and rain should be evacuated in Chaitra, Shravana, and Kartika months.
Evacuate Kapha, Vata, and Pitta rapidly during these moderate seasonal periods.
Or eliminate humors during early monsoon, autumn, and spring months.
Purify accumulated bodily wastes systematically at three-month intervals.
Counteracting extremes of cold, heat, and rain appropriately,
Administer required therapy promptly without missing the ideal therapeutic window (क्रियाकाल/treatment timing).
If no benefit occurs within seven days (सप्ताह/seven days), switch to another therapeutic line.
When prior disease urgency subsides, mixing multiple conflicting therapies is harmful.
If minor benefit is observed, continue the same therapy to achieve full therapeutic success.
For kings and rulers, medicine should be pleasing, low-volume, harmless, and pure.
Scripturally proven, highly effective, and tested multiple times.
Prepared with full dedication and approved by ministers and royal preceptors.
Tasted first by attendants and royal physician before administering to the king.
Herbs grown in a person's native habitat are most wholesome for that individual.
Even when living in another region, herbs with similar native qualities are effective.
Potent herbs properly impregnated with fresh juices (भावना/juice impregnation) become light and potent.
Rich in taste and aroma, taken in proper dose after previous meal digests,
Administered with focused mind, medicine acts like divine nectar (अमृत/divine nectar).
Thus ends the twenty-third chapter (त्रयोविंशः अध्यायः/twenty-third chapter) named 'Principles of Drug Administration and Dosages' (भेषजावचारणीयः/drug administration) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on the twofold primary therapeutic modes (द्विविध उपक्रम/twofold treatment) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
Since the subject of treatment is twofold in nature, the therapeutic measures are also declared to be twofold.
The first primary mode is nourishing therapy (संतर्पण/satiating therapy), and the second is reduction therapy (अपतर्पण/depleting therapy).
Body-building therapy (बृंहण/nourishing therapy) and lightening therapy (लंघन/lightening therapy) are stated as their synonyms.
That which promotes body bulk and weight is body-building therapy (बृंहण/body builder); that which brings lightness to body is lightening therapy (लंघन/lightening therapy).
Both these therapies are generally composed of Earth/Water or Air/Fire elemental combinations respectively.
Therapeutic unctuous therapy (स्नेहन/oleation), dryness therapy (रूक्षण/drying therapy), sudation therapy (स्वेदन/fomentation), and astringent blocking therapy (स्तंभन/astringent stanching)
Are also fundamentally classified under these two primary therapeutic modes due to elemental nature.
Furthermore, lightening reduction therapy (लंघन/lightening therapy) is classified into two types: purificatory evacuation (शोधन/cleansing) and pacifying therapy (शमन/pacification).
When a therapy forcefully expels vitiated humors out of the body,
It is declared as fivefold purificatory evacuation therapy (पंचधा शोधन/five cleansing methods).
Namely: decoction enema (निरूह/decoction enema), emesis (वमन/vomiting), purgation (विरेचन/purgation), nasal drops (शिरोविरेक/nasal cleansing), and bloodletting (रक्तमोक्षण/bloodletting).
That which neither expels humors nor excites balanced humors,
But restores equilibrium to vitiated humors is pacifying therapy (शमन/pacification), which is sevenfold.
Namely: carminative chyme digestion (पाचन/digestion), appetite boosting (दीपन/appetite kindle), hunger control (क्षुत्/fasting), thirst control (तृष्/thirst control), physical exercise (व्यायाम/exercise), sun exposure (आतप/sunbath), and wind exposure (मारुत/wind exposure).
Body-building therapy (बृंहण/nourishing therapy) acts as pacification for Vata, and also for Vata combined with Pitta.
One should administer body-building therapy to those emaciated by disease, strong medicines, alcohol, sexual indulgence, or grief (शोक/sorrow);
To those exhausted by heavy weight lifting, long journeys, chest trauma (उरःक्षत/chest injury), dry skin, or weak digestion;
To pregnant women (गर्भिणी/pregnant woman), lying-in mothers (सूतिका/puerperal mother), children, elderly persons, and all people during summer season (ग्रीष्म/summer).
Using meat (मांस/flesh), milk (क्षीर/milk), sugar (सिता/sugar), ghee (सर्पिः/ghee), and sweet unctuous enemas (मधुर बस्ति/sweet enemas);
Along with comfortable sleep, soft beds, oil massage (अभ्यंग/oil massage), baths, mental peace, and joyful atmosphere.
Patients suffering from diabetes (प्रमेह/urinary disorders), ama toxins (आमदोष/undigested chyme), over-unctuousness, fever (ज्वर/fever), thigh stiffness (ऊरुस्तंभ/thigh paralysis), or skin diseases (कुष्ठ/dermatological diseases);
Erysipelas (विसर्प/erysipelas), abscess (विद्रधि/abscess), enlarged spleen (प्लीहा/spleen), head, throat, or eye diseases;
And obese persons (स्थूल/overweight) should always be treated with lightening therapy (लंघन/lightening therapy), as well as others during late winter (शिशिर/late winter).
Among them, patients with extreme obesity, strong build, and excess Pitta and Kapha should be treated with purificatory evacuations (शोधन/cleansing).
Patients suffering from ama toxicity, fever, vomiting (छर्दि/vomiting), diarrhea (अतीसार/diarrhea), or heart diseases;
Afflicted with constipation (विबंध/constipation), heaviness, belching, or nausea, if having moderate strength, should first be treated with carminative digestion (पाचन/digestion) and appetite boosters (दीपन/appetite kindle).
Similarly, patients afflicted with these same diseases who have moderate obesity or moderate strength should be treated with carminative and digestive remedies.
When afflicted with these diseases, patients of low obesity or low strength should be treated with hunger and thirst control.
Strong patients with moderate humoral aggravation should be treated with hunger and thirst control;
And those with low strength should be treated with gentle wind, sun exposure, and physical exertion.
One should never administer body-building therapy to those fit for lightening, nor over-lighten those fit for body-building.
Applying intelligent judgment, the physician should treat them considering habitat (देश/habitat), season (काल/season), and strength (बल/vitality).
In a properly nourished person, there is strength, tissue fullness, and cure of diseases treatable by nourishment.
In a properly lightened person, there is clarity of sense organs (इन्द्रिय प्रसाद/sensory clarity), elimination of wastes, body lightness (लाघव/lightness), and good appetite (रुचि/taste);
Tolerance of hunger and thirst, purity of heart, belching, and throat;
Relief from disease severity, enthusiasm (उत्साह/enthusiasm), and disappearance of drowsiness (तंद्रा नाश/drowsiness removal).
However, if administered without considering proper quantity and rules, these therapies produce
Extreme obesity (अतिस्थौल्य/extreme obesity), extreme emaciation (अतिकार्श्य/extreme emaciation), and related diseases, which will now be stated along with their remedies.
Their clinical signs can be recognized by the symptoms of excessive nourishment and excessive lightening.
For purificatory evacuation, extreme obesity and related conditions were mentioned earlier.
Improperly digested nutrient chyme (आम/undigested chyme) mixed with heavy, unctuous Kapha humor
Circulates and loosens body tissues, bringing about excessive obesity (स्थौल्य/obesity).
Due to extreme obesity, excessive hunger, excessive thirst, profuse sweating, breathlessness, excessive sleep,
Inability to exert, physical lethargy, shortened lifespan, and loss of stamina occur.
Foul body odor (दौर्गंध्य/bad odor) and slurred speech occur due to excessive accumulation of fat tissue (मेदस्/fat tissue).
When channels are obstructed by excess fat, Vata humor residing specifically in the gut
Intensifies internal digestive fire (अग्नि/digestive fire), causing excessive hunger and thirst.
Digestive fire and Vata humor burn the obese person like forest fire burning a hollow tree.
Due to exudation from sweat-carrying vessel roots and union of fat with Kapha humor,
Profuse sweating (स्वेद बाहुल्य/excessive sweating) occurs continuously in obese individuals.
Because nutrient chyme is processed and locked in the gut due to obstructed channels,
Fat tissue alone increases excessively because it spreads everywhere in the gut.
The remaining small portion of nutrient plasma is insufficient to properly nourish blood and other tissues.
Even with equal accumulation of Vata and other humors, fat tissue accumulates much faster in a pre-disposed body.
Thus fat tissue creates imbalance among all bodily tissues.
It rapidly produces dyspnea (श्वास/asthma), fever (ज्वर/fever), abdominal enlargement (उदर/ascites), fistula-in-ano (भगंदर/fistula),
Diabetes (प्रमेह/diabetes), thigh stiffness (ऊरुस्तंभ/thigh paralysis), diabetic carbuncles (पिटिका/carbuncles), and deep abscesses (विद्रधि/abscess).
One who has excessive development of buttocks, abdomen, and breasts, and is overly obese
Is declared extremely obese (अतिस्थूल/excessively obese); for such a person, Vata-pacifying diet and therapy should be employed.
Remedies that destroy Kapha and fat tissue (मेदस्/fat tissue) should be used, such as horsegram (कुलत्थ/horsegram), wild barley, and regular barley (यव/barley);
Jurna, Shyamaka millet, mung bean (मुद्ग/green gram), medicated wines (अरिष्ट/herbal wine), and honey water (मधूदक/honey drink);
Whey (मस्तु/whey), buttermilk (तक्र/buttermilk), and sharp, warm, dry, fat-cutting medicines (छेदन भेषज/scraping drugs).
The person should engage in mental contemplation, sexual abstinence, physical exercise, purificatory cleansings, and staying awake at night.
He should practice dry baths and dry powder massages (उद्वर्तन/dry powder rubdown).
One should lick Triphala powder with honey, or Guduchi (गुडूची/giloy), Haritaki (अभया/chebulic myrobalan), and Musta (घन/nut grass);
Use of Rasanjana, Mahat Panchamula, Guggulu (गुग्गुलु/bdellium resin),
Shilajit (शिलाजतु/asphaltum), and Agnimantha juice is wholesome for obesity.
Vidanga (विडंग/false black pepper), Shunthi (नागर/dry ginger), Yavakshara (क्षार/alkali ash), iron powder (लोह रज/iron filings), honey (मधु/honey),
And barley with Amalaki powder form a formulation that overcomes extreme obesity (अतिस्थौल्य/extreme obesity).
Emetic nut (मदन/madanaphala), Triphala, Musta, along with Arishta herbal wine and whey,
Or cooked with Patha and Aragvadha, when drunk, cures diseases of excessive body-building therapy.
Similarly, Kutaja (वत्सक/conessi bark), Aragvadha (शम्याक/golden shower), Devadaru, and both turmerics;
Musta, Patha, Khadira, Triphala, Neem, and Gokshura;
Ela, Chavya, Pippali, Gajapippali, Kharashva, and Pashanabheda;
Drunk with buttermilk (तक्र/buttermilk), curd scum (दधिमंड/curd whey), or Jujube decoction,
Cures dysuria (मूत्रकृच्छ्र/painful urination), intestinal worms (कृमि/parasites), diabetes (प्रमेह/diabetes), and obesity (स्थौल्या/obesity).
Vidanga, Triphala oil, roasted barley flour (सक्तु/saktu), Trikatu, and Ajamoda
Soaked in iron-infused water and made into a drink (मंथ/soothing gruel drink), is praised for patients suffering from over-nourishment diseases.
Trikatu, Katu-rohini, Triphala, Shirisha, Vidanga, Ativisha, Shaliparni;
Hingu, Sauvarchala salt, Jeeraka, Ajamoda, Dhanyaka, Chitraka;
Both turmerics, Brihati, Kantakari, Hapusha, Patha, and Kembuka root;
Powder of these mixed with equal parts of honey, ghee, and oil,
Combined with sixteen times quantity of roasted barley flour (सक्तु/saktu), when drunk, destroys
All diseases born of extreme obesity and similar conditions;
Heart disease (हृद्रोग/cardiac disease), jaundice (कामला/jaundice), leucoderma (श्वित्र/vitiligo), dyspnea (श्वास/asthma), cough (कास/cough), and throat constriction;
Promotes intellect, intelligence, and memory, and kindles dull digestive fire.
It should be administered considering disease state, sweating, and bloodletting needs.
Extreme emaciation (अतिकार्श्य/extreme lean body) causes fatigue, cough, excessive thirst, anorexia,
Loss of unctuousness, loss of digestive fire, weakness of sight, hearing, semen, vital essence (ओजः/vital essence), hunger, and voice;
Pain in bladder, head, thighs, flanks, and sacrum, and fever (ज्वर/fever);
Delirium, upward Vata gas, exhaustion, vomiting, and tearing pain in joints and bones;
Retention of feces and urine—these arise from excessive lightening therapy (अतिलंघन/excessive fasting).
An extremely emaciated person cannot tolerate physical exertion, rain, cold, heat, hunger, thirst,
Intestinal worms, diseases, or strong medicines due to low physical strength.
Dyspnea (श्वास/asthma), cough (कास/cough), consumption (क्षय/phthisis), spleen enlargement (प्लीहा/spleen), abdominal tumor (गुल्म/phantom tumor), piles (अर्शः/hemorrhoids), and low digestive fire
Frequently afflict the emaciated person, as well as bleeding disorders (रक्तपित्त/bleeding disease) and fevers.
Emaciation (कार्श्य/leanness) is far better than obesity (स्थौल्य/obesity) because there is no easy cure for extreme obesity.
Neither simple body-building nor lightening therapy easily cures severe obesity combined with Vata.
Whereas leanness (क्रशिमा/emaciation) is easily cured by sweet, unctuous foods, comfort, and ease.
Leanness caused by excessive journeying or strain is cured by indulging in contrary wholesome comforts.
One whose buttocks, abdomen, and neck are dried up, whose joints are prominent, and whose veins are visible
Is called extremely emaciated (अतिकृश/excessively lean); for him, the earlier stated body-building regimen (बृंहण विधि/nourishing therapy) is prescribed.
Ashwagandha, Vidari, and aphrodisiac herbs (वृष्य औषध/aphrodisiac herbs) are wholesome.
With freedom from worry, joy of mind, constant nourishing food (संतर्पण/nourishing diet),
And sufficient sleep, an emaciated person gets plump like a boar.
In remaining diseases born of excessive lightening, one should apply remedies
Which provide immediate relief and long-term tissue nourishment upon habituation.
Because a person who becomes suddenly emaciated is quickly built up by body-building therapy;
While one emaciated over a long time recovers gradually through habitual nourishing regimen.
Body-building therapy should be administered assessing portion quantity (मात्रा/portion), digestive fire (अग्नि/digestive fire), and strength.
Indeed, nothing builds body bulk like meat tissue (मांस/flesh).
Meat tissue builds meat tissue because of identical tissue quality.
Therefore, meat soups of carnivorous animals (क्रव्याद्/carnivores) prepared light and slightly salty,
Vesavara spiced meats, and soups of game animals (जांगल/wild game) cooked or uncooked,
Milk preparations, and soothing nourishing drinks (तर्पण/satiating drinks) should be administered
To emaciated persons, fever patients, cough patients, and those suffering from dysuria (मूत्रकृच्छ्र/painful urination);
To those afflicted by thirst, upward Vata gas, or obstructed movement of Vata and feces.
Saktu flour mixed with equal parts of sugar, oil, honey, and ghee forms a nourishing drink (संतर्पण/satiating drink).
Similarly, a gruel drink (मंथ/soothing gruel drink) prepared with sugar, honey, wine, and roasted barley flour;
Or fanita jaggery, saktu flour, ghee, curd scum, and sour kanjika
Forms a nourishing drink that cures dysuria (मूत्रकृच्छ्र/painful urination) and upward gas (उदावर्त/upward gas).
A gruel drink prepared with dates (खर्जूर/date), raisins (मृद्वीका/raisin), Vrikshamla, Amlika, pomegranate (दाडिम/pomegranate),
Phalsa, and Amalaki instantly cures severe thirst and distress.
A gruel drink prepared sweet or sour, with water, unctuous oil, or dry,
Acts as an instant nourishing drink (मंथ/gruel drink) providing body stability, complexion, and strength.
Heavy non-nourishing diet is fit for the obese; opposite light nourishing diet is fit for the emaciated.
Preparation of barley (यव/barley) and wheat (गोधूम/wheat) should be planned appropriately for both.
Even if obesity or leanness is natural by constitution, this same therapeutic rule applies.
Because of constant disease tendency, therapy should always be administered after proper division.
One who uses body-building and lightening therapies according to proper quantity and rules
Attains balanced tissues, balanced digestive fire, and a well-compacted firm body.
With firm sensory organs and strength, he is never overcome by disease afflictions.
According to humoral movement, therapeutic modes multiply beyond two into bowel-binding, purgative, etc.
Yet fundamentally, therapeutic modes do not exceed the primary classification of two, just as all diseases do not exceed two origins.
Thus ends the twenty-fourth chapter (चतुर्विंशः अध्यायः/twenty-fourth chapter) named 'Twofold Primary Therapeutic Modes' (द्विविधोपक्रमणीयः/twofold treatment) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on oleation therapy (स्नेह विधि/unctuous therapy) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
For proper application of oleation therapy and for treating its complications (व्यापत् शमन/managing complications),
One should gather all required medicinal substances and equipment (द्रव्य संभार/medicinal supplies) beforehand.
Substances that are heavy (गुरु/heavy), cold (शीत/cooling), mobile (सर/mobile), unctuous (स्निग्ध/oily), slow (मंद/slow), subtle (सूक्ष्म/subtle), soft (मृदु/soft), and liquid (द्रव/liquid),
Are generally considered oleating medicines (स्नेहन औषध/oleating drug); those with opposite qualities cause drying (विरूक्षण/drying therapy).
Ghee (सर्पिः/clarified butter), bone marrow (मज्जा/marrow), fat (वसा/muscle fat), and oil (तैल/sesame oil) are considered supreme among unctuous substances (स्नेह/unctuous agents).
Among these, ghee (घृत/ghee) is supreme because it assimilates properties of added processing herbs (संस्कार अनुवर्तन/assimilates herb qualities) without losing its own nature.
Because of its sweet taste (माधुर्य/sweetness), non-burning nature (अविदाहित्व/non-corrosive), and familiarity since birth (जन्म सहत्व/accustomed from birth).
Ghee pacifies Pitta; oil pacifies Vata and Kapha; muscle fat and bone marrow pacify Vata in increasing order.
Sesame oil is heavier than ghee; muscle fat is heavier than oil; and bone marrow is heavier than muscle fat.
Combination of two fats is Yamaka (यमक/two-fat mix); three fats is Trivrita (त्रिवृत/three-fat mix); all four fats is Mahasneha (महास्नेह/great four-fat combination).
The primary sources of unctuous fats (स्नेहाशय/fat sources) are curd, milk, meat, bone marrow, fruits, and seeds.
Those needing sudation, purificatory evacuation, alcohol drinkers, sexually active people, athletes, and intense thinkers,
The elderly, children, weak, emaciated, dry-skinned, and those with depleted blood or semen,
Those afflicted by Vata, eye cataract (तिमिर/cataract), and severe awakening pain require oleation therapy (स्नेहन/oleation).
Oleation is contraindicated in those with extremely low digestive fire (अग्निमांद्य/low digestion), hyper-digestion, extreme obesity, or severe weakness,
Those suffering from thigh stiffness (ऊरुस्तंभ/thigh paralysis), diarrhea (अतिसार/diarrhea), ama toxicity (आमदोष/undigested chyme), throat disorders, poisoning, or ascites (उदर/dropsy),
Those afflicted by fainting, vomiting, anorexia, Kapha excess, thirst, or acute intoxication,
Women who had abnormal delivery, or those immediately undergoing nasal drops (नस्य/nasal drops), enema (बस्ती/enema), or purgation (विरेचन/purgation).
Ghee (घृत/ghee) is praised for those desiring wisdom (धी/understanding), memory (स्मृति/memory retention), intellect (मेधा/intelligence), and strong digestive fire.
Sesame oil (तैल/sesame oil) is indicated in glandular swellings, sinus tracts, worm infestations, Kapha-fat disorders, Vata ailments,
And for those desiring lightness and body firmness (दाढ्य/body firmness) or having hard bowel habits (क्रूरकोष्ठ/hard bowels).
Muscle fat (वसा/muscle fat) is indicated for those depleted by wind, sun exposure, long walks, heavy loads, sexual activity, or physical exercise,
Those with dry tissues, high pain tolerance, hyper-active digestion, or channels blocked by Vata humor.
Bone marrow (मज्जा/marrow) is used for pain in joints, bones, vital spots (मर्म/vital organs), and internal organs (कोष्ठ/viscera),
As well as for burns, trauma, prolapsed uterus (योनिभ्रंश/uterine prolapse), earache, and severe headache.
Sesame oil should be taken in rainy season (प्रावृट्/early monsoon); ghee in autumn (शरद्/autumn); muscle fat and marrow in spring (वसंत/spring).
All fats may be administered in any season when the sky is clear, bright, and free of clouds.
In moderate seasons, when humors are balanced, or in Vata-Kapha and Kapha states, administer fat during daytime.
Administer fat at night in Vata and Pitta conditions, or in dual combinations dominated by Pitta humor.
In emergency cold conditions, administer oil during daytime; in emergency hot conditions, administer ghee at night.
Reversing timing based on humors and seasonal heat or cold prevents adverse complications.
Taking fat at wrong time causes Vata-Kapha diseases at night and Pitta diseases during daytime.
Oleation fat may be administered skillfully through foods, edibles, enemas (बस्ती/enema),
Nasal drops (नस्य/nasal administration), oil massage (अभ्यंग/massage), gargling (गंडूष/gargle), head oiling (मूर्द्धतैल/head oiling), and eye drops (अक्षितर्पण/eye bath).
There are 64 combinations (चतुःषष्टि विचारणा/64 fat preparations) combining fats with various tastes and food items.
These dietary fat preparations are mild because fat is mixed and overpowered by food ingredients.
Unmixed pure fat intake (अच्छपेय/pure fat drinking) is the supreme method when no special contraindications exist.
Pure fat intake is best because it accomplishes oleation action rapidly and powerfully.
Dosage amounts are classified into short, medium, and maximum doses which digest in 2, 4, or 8 praharas (6, 12, or 24 hours).
These are small, medium, and maximum doses; a very small trial dose should be evaluated first.
One should examine humor strength and digestion power, starting with a minimal test dose.
Administering a large dose without knowing bowel habits (कोष्ठ/bowel nature) can endanger the patient's life.
For weak persons, low digestive fire, children, elderly, delicate individuals,
Those with unaccustomed empty stomach, fever (ज्वर/fever), diarrhea (अतिसार/diarrhea), or cough (कास/cough),
A small dose (ह्रस्व मात्रा/small dose) is easily drinkable, safe, and easily managed during regimen.
Though digesting slowly, it gives strength, causes no pain, and prevents complications even if altered.
For those suffering from diabetes (मूर्छ मेह/urinary disease), boils, skin diseases (कुष्ठ/skin disease), or gout (वातशोणित/gout),
A medium dose (मध्यम मात्रा/medium dose) is easily digestable for medium bowel habits (मृदुकोष्ठ/soft bowels).
It does not sap strength, causes little distress, and is sufficient for purificatory oleation.
For those with robust body, strong digestive fire, great strength, high tolerance for hunger and thirst,
Afflicted by abdominal tumors (गुल्म/tumor), abdominal distension, erysipelas (विसर्प/erysipelas), or snakebite,
Or insanity and dysuria, a maximum dose (महती मात्रा/large dose) acts rapidly.
Spreading through all body channels, a properly administered large dose overcomes severe diseases.
For purificatory oleation (शोधन अर्थ/cleansing purpose), a large unmixed fat dose should be drunk early morning after yesterday's meal is fully digested.
For pacifying oleation (शमन अर्थ/pacifying purpose), a medium dose without food when hungry is praised.
For nourishing oleation (बृंहण अर्थ/nourishing purpose), a small fat dose mixed with meat broth or wine during meals is wholesome.
In children, elderly, thirsty, those averse to fat, habitual wine drinkers,
Women, those constantly taking fat, low digestive fire, delicate, or afraid of pain,
Soft bowels, mild humor aggravation, hot season, and emaciated persons,
Fat taken before meals strengthens thighs, legs, and waist (कटी/waist).
It directs humors downward (अनुलोमन/downward movement), promotes health, and cures lower body diseases.
Fat taken in middle of meal increases body bulk, digestive power, stability, and cures middle abdominal pain.
Fat taken after meal stabilizes sensory organs and cures diseases above the clavicle (ऊर्ध्वजत्रु/head and neck).
For Vata aggravation, ghee with rock salt is recommended; for Pitta, pure unmixed ghee is desired.
For Kapha excess, the physician should administer ghee mixed with alkali (क्षार/alkali) and Trikatu spices.
Warm water (उष्ण जल/warm water) should be drunk after drinking pure fat to facilitate smooth digestion,
And to cleanse greasy mouth coating, except when taking Tuvaraka or Bhallataka oil.
Warm regimen should be followed during oleation because fat digestion is obstructed by coldness.
One should wear light warm clothes, sit in a windless room, and await full digestion.
If thirsty, one should drink warm water while awaiting complete digestion of fat.
Headache, dizziness, spitting, fainting, exhaustion, and discomfort indicate fat is currently digesting.
Lightness, comfort, and relief of symptoms indicate that fat has been completely digested.
Downward gas movement, feeling healthy, hunger, thirst, and clean belching confirm complete digestion.
In case of doubt between digestion and indigestion, one should drink warm water again.
If belching becomes clear, appetite returns, and body feels light, digestion is complete.
The diet before, during, and after oleation therapy should be warm, liquid, non-clogging,
Neither too unctuous nor mixed with opposing items, taken in measured portion.
The patient should use warm water, observe celibacy (ब्रह्मचर्य/celibacy), and sleep on a soft bed.
Avoid physical exercise, suppression of natural urges, grief, rain, snow, sun exposure,
Drafts of wind, riding vehicles, long walks, loud talking, excessive eating, and improper sitting.
Avoid low or high pillows, dust, smoke, and day sleeping for as many days as fat was taken.
One should observe these restrictions for an equal number of days after stopping fat intake.
This general protective regimen applies to all purificatory therapies and emaciated patients.
The protective care during pacifying oleation is similar to that following purgation therapy.
Before administering oleation fat, a mild appetized medicine should be given,
To kindle digestive fire and bring lightness to stomach and intestines.
Pure fat should be drunk for 3 days in soft bowels, and up to 7 days in hard bowels.
Or until proper unctuous signs appear; fat should not be taken beyond 7 days as it becomes habitual (सात्म्य/accustomed).
When fat becomes accustomed, it no longer excites or mobilizes morbid humors.
Or excessive oleation causes severe complications, just as excessive water breaks a dam,
Washing away the soil and destroying the earthen bank completely,
Similarly, excessive fat overpowers digestive fire and causes tissue breakdown.
Proper oleation signs: downward gas movement, bright digestive fire, unctuous un-clotted stool,
Soft smooth skin, mild fatigue, aversion to fat, and feeling lightness in body.
Clarity of sensory organs confirms ideal oleation; dry skin and hard stool indicate under-oleation.
Over-oleation signs: paleness (पांडु/paleness), body weakness, nasal and rectal discharge,
Rectal burning, indigestion, vomiting, fainting, thirst, and tenesmus (प्रवाहिका/tenesmus),
Dry belching, dizziness, dyspnea, and cough caused by excessive fat intake.
Fat taken in improper dose, unwholesome diet, wrong season, or with improper habits,
Causes swelling, piles (अर्शस्/hemorrhoids), drowsiness, deep sleep, loss of consciousness,
Itching, skin diseases (कुष्ठ/skin disease), fever, nausea, abdominal colic, and bloating.
Weakness of stomach and sense organs, stiffness, and voice obstruction,
Along with various humor-specific disorders caused by excessive oleation.
Its management: fasting, thirst control, vomiting, sudation, dry drinks, and dry medicines.
Buttermilk (तक्र/buttermilk), Arishta wines, coarse grains, barley (यव/barley), Shyamaka, Kodrava,
Pippali, Triphala, honey (क्षौद्र/honey), Haritaki, cow urine (गोमूत्र/cow urine), and Guggulu,
Applied appropriately according to specific complications of oleation.
Drying therapy (विरूक्षण/drying therapy) features signs similar to lightening therapy (लंघन/lightening).
When digestive fire is intensely flare-up by fat in Pitta constitution,
It rapidly burns the fat, consumes vital essence (ओजः/vital essence), and spreads through body,
Producing severe burning heat, complications, and intense thirst.
Such a patient should not drink cold water suddenly as it can endanger life;
Cold sprinkling, cold baths, and cold applications should be used to pacify thirst.
The patient feels burning heat from fat-fire like a serpent burning in its own venom.
If indigestion is severe, cold water should be sprinkled over head and face;
If un-pacified, induce vomiting by making patient drink warm water.
In Kapha-Vata indigestion, induce vomiting after eating dry food mass.
When humors are balanced, wash out residual fat completely using warm water.
Then adopt the previously stated protective regimen according to humor strength.
Pure ghee should never be given in Pitta conditions accompanied by ama toxicity (साम/undigested chyme),
Because it afflicts the entire body, destroys consciousness, and leads to death.
After proper oleation, the patient eating warm liquid soup from game meat should undergo sudation.
The well-oleated and sudated patient should be given purgation, or emesis after 1 day.
For 1 day prior to emesis, excite Kapha humor using Kapha-increasing food items,
Such as sesame, black gram (माष/black gram), curd, milk, jaggery, and fish soup.
For muscular, obese, high Kapha, irregular digestion, and fat-accustomed persons,
Apply drying therapy (विरूक्षण/drying) first, followed by oleation and purificatory evacuation.
This approach prevents oleation complications and mobilizes morbid humors effectively.
Unctuous fat that has become accustomed (असात्म्य/unaccustomed) fails to mobilize morbid wastes.
In children, elderly, and those unable to observe strict oleation restrictions,
Instant oleating preparations (सद्यः स्नेह/instant oleation) should be administered without distress.
Rich meat decoctions from game or aquatic animals,
Or thick gruel (यवागू/thick gruel) fried in abundant ghee/oil,
Sesame powder mixed with fat, jaggery, and Krisara gruel,
Sesame soup with abundant fat, or rice gruel prepared with ghee,
Warm milk gruel served in a vessel with sugar and ghee,
Or milk mixed with ghee and rock salt acts as instant oleating agent.
Pancha-prasrita gruel cooked with 5 measures of fats and 1 measure of rice,
Milk pudding (पायस/milk pudding) mixed with black gram and abundant fat,
Oil with ginger and jaggery taken before meals by meat eaters,
Fat taken with clear wine (सुरा/clear wine) or curd supernatant with jaggery,
Pork fat (वसा/muscle fat), ghee, Pippali, rock salt, and sesame seeds,
Pippali, rock salt, four fats (चतुःस्नेह/four fats mix), and curd whey (मस्तु/whey),
Ghee processed with curds, Trikatu, Amalaki, and raisin juice,
Ghee processed with barley, jujube, horsegram, alkali, milk, and curd,
Taken in equal parts acts as an excellent instant oleating formulation.
Four fats processed with jujube and Triphala decoction cure gynecological and seminal diseases,
Serving as potent instant oleating preparations.
Fats combined with rock salt (लवण/rock salt) produce rapid oleation in human body,
Because salt is liquefying, non-dry, subtle, hot in potency, and rapidly pervading.
Jaggery, marshy meat, milk, sesame, black gram, wine, and curd,
Should not be used for oleation in skin diseases, edema, or diabetes.
For these patients, fats processed with Triphala, Pippali, Haritaki, and Guggulu should be used.
These processed medicated fats cause no adverse complications in such diseases,
And nourish tissues in those emaciated by chronic illnesses.
A person who habitually practices proper oleation gains bright internal digestive fire, clean channels,
Fresh tissue elements, strength, glowing complexion, firm senses, delayed aging, and lives a full hundred years.
Thus ends the twenty-fifth chapter (पंचविंशः अध्यायः/twenty-fifth chapter) named 'Oleation Therapy' (स्नेहविधिः/oleation rules) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on sudation and fomentation therapy (स्वेद विधि/fomentation rules) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
Fomentation with direct fire (अग्नि स्वेद/fire-based sudation) is categorized into four types.
Namely: conduction heat (ताप/direct thermal heat), warm poultice (उपनाह/warm poultice), liquid pouring (द्रव/herbal liquid pour), and steam vapor (ऊष्मा/herbal steam).
Thermal conduction heat (ताप स्वेद/conduction heat) should be applied directly using palms (पाणि/hands), bronze plates (कांस्य/bronze vessel), hot iron plates, hot sand (वालुका/heated sand), warm cloth (वस्त्र/cloth), hot clay pots (घटिका/earthen pot), or direct flame.
Warm poultice fomentation (उपनाह स्वेद/warm poultice) is prepared using fragrant herbs (सर्वगंध/all aromatics), grains, mustard (सर्षप/mustard seeds), fermenting yeast (किण्व/yeast), Vacha, Devadaru, Rasna, Erandamula, Shatahva, and Vata-pacifying herb powders, mixed with barley and wheat coarse flour, marshy animal fat, bile, head/foot meats, and minced spiced meat (वेसवार/minced spiced meat).
When Vata is combined with Kapha, use Surasadi herbs; when combined with Pitta, use Padmakadi cooling herbs.
Mixed individually or together with milk (क्षीर/milk), vinegar (धान्याम्ल/sour rice gruel), rock salt (लवण/rock salt), and curd whey (मस्तु/whey), applied warm and unctuous, bound repeatedly with warm non-foul soft hairy animal skins, or with thick Vata-pacifying leaves, silk, or wool fabrics; applied during day and untied at night to prevent skin burning, or vice versa according to disease, seat, and season.
Liquid fomentation (द्रव स्वेद/liquid fomentation) is twofold: pouring/shower (परिषेक/liquid shower) and tub immersion (अवगाह/tub immersion).
Prepared with decoctions of Shigru, Varuna, Amrita, Mulaka, Mustard, Surasa, Arjaka, Vasa, Bamboo, Ashmantaka, Ashoka, Shirisha, Arka, Karanja, Eranda, Malati leaves, and Dashamula Vata-pacifying herbs, mixed with sour milk, vinegar, or milk; filled into pitchers or shower pipes, poured over the patient's body anointed with medicated oil, while sitting or lying on a mat, covered with cloth.
Tub immersion fomentation is performed by immersing the patient in a large tub, vat, or trough (द्रोणी/immersion tub) filled with the same warm herbal decoctions.
Vapor steam fomentation (ऊष्म स्वेद/steam vapor) is classified into eight distinct forms.
Namely: Pinda (bolus), Samstara (bedding), Nadi (pipe steam), Ghanashma (heated stone slab), Kumbhi (earthen pot), Koopa (pit steam), Kuti (cottage steam), and Jentaka (sweat chamber).
Red-hot clay tiles, stones, mud clods, or iron boluses are held with tongs and dipped in sour liquid vinegar.
Wrapped in wet cloth, applied over painful body parts, nodes, or Kapha-fat dominant swellings to induce sweating.
Or using warm bags of heated sand, cow dung, grain husks, or warm pastes, rice puddings (पायस/rice pudding), or meat pastes wrapped in cloth; this is called Pinda sveda or Sankara sveda.
It is also known as Sankara sveda (संकर स्वेद/mixed bolus sudation).
Boiling sudation herbs in a pot, spreading them warm on a bed covered with silk, wool, or Vata-pacifying leaves, and covering the lying patient with blankets to induce sweating; this is Samstara sveda.
Boiling herbs in a closed pot, connecting a curved hollow pipe made of elephant-trunk shape, bamboo, or Karanja leaves, and directing gentle steam onto the patient's oil-anointed body; this is Nadi sveda.
Because steam flowing through a curved pipe loses harsh impact and gently sweats the skin without burning.
Heating a large stone slab or ground with Vata-pacifying wood fire, removing embers, sprinkling with warm water or vinegar, spreading cloth/leaves, and having the patient lie down to sweat; this is Ghanashma sveda.
Boiling herbal decoctions in an earthen pot buried underground or placed under a slatted seat, letting the rising steam sweat the sitting patient; this is Kumbhi sveda.
Or dropping red-hot iron balls or clay tiles into the buried pot containing herbal liquids so rising steam sweeps the body.
Digging a pit beneath a bed, filling it with burning smokeless Vata-pacifying wood embers, and sweating the lying patient above; this is Koopa sveda.
Constructing a circular windowless cottage coated with herbal pastes, placing smokeless charcoal stoves around a central bed, and sweating the patient inside; this is Kuti sveda.
To construct a Jentaka sweat chamber, examine and select a suitable ground on eastern or northern side.
On fertile black or golden soil near a lake or river bank, lay out a circular or square plot of 8 cubits facing east or north.
Build a circular mud structure 16 cubits high and wide, well-plastered with multiple small ventilation windows.
Construct a continuous raised earthen bench 1 cubit high and wide along the inner perimeter wall up to the door.
Build a central clay oven column 2 cubits wide with numerous fine air holes and a fitted lid.
Fill the central oven with Khadira or Asana hardwood and burn until smokeless red-hot embers remain.
When the wood is thoroughly burnt, smokeless, and the chamber is uniformly heated with gentle steam heat,
The patient, anointed with Vata-pacifying oil and wrapped in cloth, should enter the chamber.
The physician instructs: 'O gentle one, enter for your health and complete cure.'
Lie down comfortably on the raised bench on your side or back.
Do not leave or step off this bench even if overcome by heavy sweating or dizziness, until full breath recovery.
Stepping down from the bench in a dizzy sweated state causes collapse and instant loss of life.
Therefore, under no circumstances should you leave the raised bench.
When you feel all bodily moisture melted, clear unctuous sweat flowing freely, all channels opened, body light, and stiffness/pain removed,
Then follow the raised bench pathway toward the door and step out.
After exiting, do not immediately enter cold water or cold air, to protect your eyesight.
After resting a moment until heat and exhaustion subside, bathe in pleasant warm water and take wholesome food; this is Jentaka sveda.
Thermal heat and steam vapor (ताप ऊष्म स्वेद/thermal steam) are indicated specifically for Kapha disorders.
Warm poultice (उपनाह/poultice) is indicated for Vata disorders; liquid pour (द्रव/liquid pour) is indicated when Pitta is combined.
Non-fire sudation (अनाग्नि स्वेद/non-fire sudation) is indicated when Vata is obstructed by Kapha.
Namely: windless room (निवात/windless room), heavy blankets (गुरु प्रावरण/heavy blanket), physical exercise (व्यायाम/exercise), hunger/fasting (क्षुध्/fasting), sun exposure (आतप/sunbath), wrestling (नियुद्ध/wrestling), walking/load carrying (अध्व भार/load carrying), anger (अमर्ष/anger), and alcohol drinking (मद्य पान/wine drinking).
Poultice without direct fire heat is used when Pitta is associated following the same rules.
Here are summary verses on fomentation therapy:
One should undergo sudation in a draft-free room after internal/external oleation and after previous meal digests.
Sudation intensity should be strong, medium, or mild based on disease, patient strength, land, and season.
Use dry sudation (रूक्ष स्वेद/dry sudation) for Kapha; unctuous sudation for Vata; dry-unctuous for Kapha-Vata combined.
When Vata resides in stomach (आमाशय/stomach), apply dry sudation first; when Kapha resides in colon (पक्वाशय/colon), apply unctuous sudation first.
Follow dry or unctuous sequence according to the primary anatomical seat of the humor.
Apply very mild sudation on groin (वंक्षण/groin); apply minimal or no heat over eyes (दृश्/eyes), testicles (मुष्क/scrotum), and heart (हृदय/chest).
Cover the patient's eyes with fresh lotus leaves, blue waterlilies, or cool dough balls before applying heat.
Cool the heart region repeatedly using pearl necklaces, lotus flowers, or cold water-soaked hands.
Frequently touch the chest area with cool wet hands during sweating.
When coldness and pain subside and limbs become soft and flexible, sudation is complete.
Massage gently, bathe in warm water, and then follow post-oleation dietary regimen.
Pitta-blood aggravation, excessive thirst, fainting, voice loss, physical weakness, dizziness,
Joint pain, fever, reddish-dark skin patches, and vomiting indicate excessive sudation.
In over-sudation (स्वेदातियोग/excessive sudation), apply stanching cooling therapies (स्तंभन/astringent cooling therapy).
In poisoning, alkali burn, severe diarrhea, vomiting, or fainting, stanching therapy is indicated.
Sudation drugs are heavy, sharp, and hot; stanching drugs are opposite in nature.
Sudation drugs are mobile, unctuous, sharp, and light; stanching drugs are stable, dry, smooth, and heavy.
Stanching medicines are smooth, dry, subtle, mobile, liquid, bitter, astringent, and sweet in taste.
Stanching remedies are primarily bitter, astringent, and sweet in taste.
Successful stanching restores physical strength and relieves disease symptoms.
Stiffness of skin, body rigidity, chest oppression, and jaw lock indicate excessive stanching.
Rigidity of feet, lips, and skin with dark patches confirms over-stanching.
Do not sweat extremely obese, dry, weak, fainting, or unconscious individuals.
Patients with severe chest injury, emaciation, alcohol intoxication, or bleeding disorders.
Patients suffering from cataract (तिमिर/cataract), abdominal enlargement (उदर/dropsy), erysipelas (विसर्प/erysipelas), or skin leprosy.
Those who have just taken milk, curd, oil, or wine, or undergone purgation.
Persons suffering from burns, heat exhaustion, anger, grief, or fear.
Patients with hunger, thirst, jaundice, anemia, diabetes, or severe Pitta affliction.
Pregnant women, menstruating women, and puerperal women—except mild heat in emergencies.
Indicated in dyspnea (श्वास/asthma), cough (कास/cough), rhinitis (प्रतिश्याया/cold), hiccups (हिध्मा/hiccup), bloating, and constipation.
Hoarseness of voice, Vata disorders, hemiplegia (पक्षाघात/paralysis), and tetanus (अपतानक/tetanus).
Bodyache, waist pain (कटी शूल/waist pain), flank pain, back pain, abdominal colic, and jaw stiffness.
Scrotal enlargement (मुष्क वृद्धि/scrotal swelling), cramps, spasms, and ankle sprains (वातकंटक/ankle sprain).
Dysuria (मूत्रकृच्छ्र/dysuria), tumors (अर्बुद/tumor), glandular swellings, seminal obstruction, and stiff thighs.
Tremors (वेपथु/tremors), edema (शोथ/edema), numbness (स्वाप/numbness), stiffness, yawning, and heaviness.
Pain in ears, neck, head, GI tract, calves, feet, and thighs.
Apply appropriate sudation therapy selecting suitable drugs according to the specific disease.
After sweating, eat wholesome food matching the humor and disease state.
On the day of sudation, anoint the body with oil and strictly avoid physical exercise.
Sudation kindles digestive fire (अग्नि दीप्ति/digestion boost), softens tissues, clears skin glow, improves appetite, and purifies bodily channels.
It relieves lethargy, drowsiness, stiffness, and restores instant mobility to rigid joints.
Dormant toxins and humors adhering to channels, tissues, GI tract, or joints are liquefied by heat.
Once liquefied and brought into the GI tract, they are completely eliminated by purificatory cleansings.
Thus ends the twenty-sixth chapter (षड्विंशः अध्यायः/twenty-sixth chapter) named 'Sudation and Fomentation Therapy' (स्वेदविधिः/fomentation rules) of Ashtanga Sangraha.




Now, therefore, we shall explain the chapter (अध्याय/section) on emetic and purgative purification therapies (वमन विरेचन विधि/emesis and purgation rules) of Ashtanga Sangraha.
Eliminating humors upward through the mouth is called emetic therapy (वमन/emesis); eliminating downward through rectum is called purgative therapy (विरेचन/purgation); both are called evacuations because they purge waste (मल विरेचन/waste evacuation).
Warm, sharp, subtle, fast-spreading, and tissue-softening drugs reach the heart by their potency, spread through channels, soften unctuous tissues, liquefy humor accumulations inside organs like heat melting moist wood, and draw them from macro and micro channels.
By their sharpness and tissue-softening nature, these drugs detach deep-seated morbid humors from tissue channels.
The detached, floating mass of humors, slipping smoothly like water in an oiled vessel without adhering, reaches the stomach; driven by Udana Vayu, drugs rich in Fire and Air elements move upward due to upward potency, while drugs rich in Water and Earth move downward.
Drugs containing dual elemental qualities perform both upward and downward evacuations due to dual potency.
When Kapha is aggravated or mixed with Pitta in stomach, emesis (वमन/emesis) is indicated; when Pitta is aggravated or mixed with Kapha in intestine, purgation (विरेचन/purgation) is indicated.
Emetic therapy is indicated in poisoning, snakebites, toxic arrows, incompatible food, indigestion, acute fever, consumption, diarrhea, downward bleeding, gastroenteritis, anorexia, goiter, tumors, elephantiasis, obesity, mania, epilepsy, asthma, cough, nausea, skin diseases, anemia, and Kapha disorders.
Just as water clears weeds from paddy fields, emesis eradicates Kapha disorders from their very root.
Emesis is contra-indicated in pregnant women, delicate persons, busy workers, dry persons, hyperactive digestive fire, daily fatigued laborers, injured emaciated, elderly, children, weak, fainting, hungry, thirsty, those suffering from acute vomiting, upward bleeding, Vata fits, eye/ear pain, and worm infestations.
Except in acute poisoning, food toxicity, and emergency indigestion where immediate emesis is life-saving despite contraindications.
In pregnant women, emesis causes fetal distress, abortion (गर्भस्राव/miscarriage), and severe complications.
In delicate persons, violent strain causes severe bleeding upward or downward.
In distracted busy workers, emetic medicine fails to act properly.
Incomplete or strained emesis causes retention of morbid humors and complications.
In dry persons, emesis provokes Vata, causing body stiffness and cramps.
In persons accustomed to dry food, Vata depletes humors further, causing severe loss of strength.
In hyperactive digestive fire, strong emetic medicine gets digested by digestive fire rather than causing evacuation.
In daily fatigued travelers and heavy lifters, emesis causes extreme exhaustion.
In chest-injured persons, violent strain exacerbates internal chest bleeding.
In severely emaciated persons, drug strength overpowers body strength, causing risk of fatal injury.
In continuous vomiting and upward bleeding, emesis forces Udana Vayu upward, risking fatal hemorrhage or loss of life.
In patients with upward Vata or recent enema, emesis causes severe reverse gas movement.
In hard bowels or difficult vomit, violent strain causes internal humor stagnation, leading to erysipelas, stiffness, stupor, or death.
In cardiac patients, emetic strain causes acute heart failure.
In patients with intestinal reversal or facial paralysis, emetic strain aggravates pain or proves fatal.
In worm infestation without prior enema, emetic strain causes worms to migrate upward, choking throat and heart.
Worried patients and those undergoing nasal/smoking therapies require careful handling.
In indigestion, all purifications except mild emesis are avoided to prevent toxic ama generation.
In fresh fever, purificatory evacuations are avoided to prevent locking immature humors in tissues.
Purgation therapy (विरेचन/purgation) is indicated in chronic fever, upward bleeding, abdominal tumors, spleen disease, piles, fistula, ascites, worms, dysuria, seminal/uterine disorders, gout, jaundice, skin diseases, cataracts, and Pitta disorders.
Just as removing heat quenches a burning house, purgation completely eradicates Pitta disorders from the body.
Purgation is contra-indicated in fresh fever, acute diarrhea, downward bleeding, rectal ulceration, recent fasting, night vigil, low digestion, consumption, alcoholism, bloating, trauma, and hard bowel.
In fresh fever, purgative drugs fail to digest immature humors and provoke severe Vata.
In acute diarrhea and downward bleeding, purgation causes fatal fluid loss.
In rectal ulceration, purgation causes excruciating rectal pain and collapse.
Fasted or exhausted persons cannot tolerate the force of purgative medicine.
In tuberculosis, tissue stool waste sustains body strength; purging waste causes collapse of life.
In alcoholism, depleted tissues combined with purgation provoke Vata to endanger life.
In abdominal flatulence, purgation forces trapped gas into tissues, causing severe colic or death.
In traumatic injury or internal wound, purgation provokes Vata and destroys vital breath.
In hard bowel persons, mild purgative medicine gets digested without purging, causing toxic buildup.
In hyperactive digestive fire, purgative medicine gets burnt up without clearing bowel.
In persons with dry tissues, purgation causes severe gas retention, cramps, and weakness.
Except in emergencies like acute poisoning or severe toxic indigestion where immediate purgation is mandatory.
A patient suitable for emesis should first be properly oleated with medicated ghee for 3-7 days and sudated.
On the day prior to emesis, feed the patient Kapha-provoking unctuous food like meat broth, milk, curds, sesame, and black gram to excite Kapha in stomach.
On the morning of emesis, after previous meal is fully digested and morning Kapha time arrives, perform light sudation.
Administer emetic formulation made with Madanaphala (मदनफल/emetic nut), Yashtimadhu, Vacha, or honey with warm milk or gruel.
Make the patient sit comfortably on a knee-high seat, encourage him, and wait for emetic urge to arise.
As perspiration appears on forehead, it indicates humor liquefaction; goosebumps indicate humors shifting from channels; abdominal distension indicates humors reaching stomach; nausea indicates upward movement.
When urge arises, bend forward, support head and sides, tickle throat gently with finger or lotus stalk, and vomit comfortably.
Vomit without violent straining, allowing the natural force of emetic medicine to expel Kapha and Pitta.
Observe the expelled matter: first comes emetic drug, then Kapha, then Pitta, and finally small Vata gas.
Signs of proper emesis (सम्यक् वमन/proper emesis): sequential expulsion of Kapha and Pitta, lightness of chest and head, clarity of senses, and freedom from nausea.
Signs of insufficient emesis (अयोग/insufficient emesis): lack of proper urge, expulsion of drug alone, itching, skin eruptions, heaviness, and sticky throat.
Signs of excessive emesis (अतियोग/excessive emesis): expulsion of blood, froth, intense thirst, fainting, throat dryness, and severe Vata fits.
After proper emesis, wash hands, feet, and face with warm water, inhale medicated smoke (धूम/herbal smoke), and rest in a windless room.
